## Abstract

The Fetch standard defines requests, responses, and the process that binds them:
fetching.

## Goals

The goal is to unify fetching across the web platform and provide consistent handling
ofeverything that involves, including:

- URL schemes     
- Redirects     
- Cross-origin semantics     
- CSP [[CSP]](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")     
- Fetch Metadata [[FETCH-METADATA]](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers")     
- Service workers [[SW]](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly")     
- Mixed Content [[MIX]](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content")     
- Upgrade Insecure Requests [[UPGRADE-INSECURE-REQUESTS]](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests")     
- \``Referer`\` [[REFERRER]](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy")   

To do so it also supersedes the HTTP \`[`Origin`](#http-origin)\` header semanticsoriginally defined in
*The Web Origin Concept*. [[ORIGIN]](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept")

## 1. Preface

At a high level, fetching a resource is a fairly simple operation. A request goes
in, aresponse comes out. The details of that operation arehowever quite involved
and used to not be written down carefully and differ from one APIto the next.

Numerous APIs provide the ability to fetch a resource, e.g. HTML’s `img` and`script` element,
CSS' `cursor` and `list-style-image`,the `navigator.sendBeacon()` and `self.importScripts()` JavaScriptAPIs. The Fetch Standard provides a unified
architecture for these features so they areall consistent when it comes to various
aspects of fetching, such as redirects and theCORS protocol.

The Fetch Standard also defines the [`fetch()`](#dom-global-fetch) JavaScript API, whichexposes most of the networking functionality at a fairly low level of abstraction.

## 2. Infrastructure

This specification depends on the Infra Standard. [[INFRA]](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard")

This specification uses terminology from *ABNF*, *Encoding*,*HTML*, *HTTP*, *MIME
Sniffing*, *Streams*,*URL*, *Web IDL*, *WebSockets*, and *WebTransport*.[[ABNF]](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF")[[ENCODING]](#biblio-encoding "Encoding Standard")[[HTML]](#biblio-html "HTML Standard")[[HTTP]](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics")[[MIMESNIFF]](#biblio-mimesniff "MIME Sniffing Standard")[[STREAMS]](#biblio-streams "Streams Standard")[[URL]](#biblio-url "URL Standard")[[WEBIDL]](#biblio-webidl "Web IDL Standard")[[WEBSOCKETS]](#biblio-websockets "WebSockets Standard")[[WEBTRANSPORT]](#biblio-webtransport "WebTransport")

*ABNF* means ABNF as augmented by HTTP (in particular the addition of `#`)and RFC
7405. [[RFC7405]](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF")

---

*Credentials* are HTTP cookies, TLS client certificates, and [authentication entries](#authentication-entry) (for HTTP authentication). [[COOKIES]](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism")[[TLS]](#biblio-tls "The Transport Layer Security (TLS) Protocol Version 1.3") [[HTTP]](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics")

---

A *fetch params* is a [struct](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct) used as a bookkeeping detail by the[fetch](#concept-fetch) algorithm. It has the following [items](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item):
*request*     A [request](#concept-request).     *process request body chunk
length* (default null)     *process request end-of-body* (default null)     *process
early hints response* (default null)     *process response* (default null)     *process
response end-of-body* (default null)     *process response consume body* (default
null)     Null or an algorithm.     *task destination* (default null)     Null, a
[global object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object), or a [parallel queue](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue).    
*cross-origin isolated capability* (default false)     A boolean.     *controller*
(default a new [fetch controller](#fetch-controller))     A [fetch controller](#fetch-controller).    
*timing info*     A [fetch timing info](#fetch-timing-info).     *preloaded response
candidate* (default null)     Null, "`pending`", or a [response](#concept-response).    

A *fetch controller* is a [struct](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct) used to enable callers of[fetch](#concept-fetch) to perform certain operations on it after it has started. It has the following[items](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item):
*state* (default "`ongoing`")     "`ongoing`", "`terminated`", or "`aborted`"     *full timing info* (default
null)     Null or a [fetch timing info](#fetch-timing-info).     *report timing steps*
(default null)     Null or an algorithm accepting a [global object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object).    
*serialized abort reason* (default null)     Null or a [Record](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type) (result of [StructuredSerialize](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize)).    
*next manual redirect steps* (default null)     Null or an algorithm accepting nothing.


To *report timing* for a[fetch controller](#fetch-controller) controller given a
[global object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object) global:


1.       [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert): controller’s [report timing steps](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps) is non-null.

2.       Call controller’s [report timing steps](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps) with global.


To *process the next manual redirect* for a[fetch controller](#fetch-controller) controller:


1.       [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert): controller’s [next manual redirect steps](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps) is non-null.

2.       Call controller’s [next manual redirect steps](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps).


To*extract full timing info*given a [fetch controller](#fetch-controller) controller:


1.       [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert): controller’s [full timing info](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info) is non-null.

2.       Return controller’s [full timing info](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info).


To *abort* a [fetch controller](#fetch-controller)controller with an optional error:


1.       Set controller’s [state](#fetch-controller-state) to "`aborted`".

2.       Let fallbackError be an "`[AbortError](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror)`" `[DOMException](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException)`.

3.       Set error to fallbackError if it is not given.

4.       Let serializedError be [StructuredSerialize](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize)(error). If that threw an exception, catch it, and let serializedError be [StructuredSerialize](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize)(fallbackError).

5.       Set controller’s [serialized abort reason](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason) to serializedError.


To *deserialize a serialized abort reason*, given null or a
[Record](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type)abortReason
and a [realm](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm) realm:


1.       Let fallbackError be an "`[AbortError](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror)`" `[DOMException](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException)`.

2.       Let deserializedError be fallbackError.

3.       If abortReason is non-null, then set deserializedError to [StructuredDeserialize](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize)(abortReason, realm). If that threw an exception or returned undefined, then set deserializedError to fallbackError.

4.       Return deserializedError.


To *terminate* a [fetch controller](#fetch-controller)controller, set controller’s
[state](#fetch-controller-state) to"`terminated`".

A [fetch params](#fetch-params) fetchParams is *aborted* ifits [controller](#fetch-params-controller)’s [state](#fetch-controller-state)
is"`aborted`".

A [fetch params](#fetch-params) fetchParams is *canceled* ifits [controller](#fetch-params-controller)’s [state](#fetch-controller-state)
is"`aborted`" or "`terminated`".

A *fetch timing info* is a [struct](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct) used to maintain timinginformation needed by
*Resource Timing* and *Navigation Timing*. It has thefollowing [items](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item): [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing") [[NAVIGATION-TIMING]](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing")
*start time* (default 0)     *redirect start time* (default 0)     *redirect
end time* (default 0)     *post-redirect start time* (default 0)     *final service
worker start time* (default 0)     *final network-request start time* (default 0)
*first interim network-response start time* (default 0)     *final network-response
start time* (default 0)     *end time* (default 0)     A
`[DOMHighResTimeStamp](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp)`.
*final connection timing info* (default null)     Null or a [connection timing info](#connection-timing-info).    
*service worker timing info* (default null)     Null or a [service worker timing info](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info).    
*server-timing headers* (default « »)     A [list](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list) of strings.    
*render-blocking* (default false)     A boolean.      
A *response body info* is a [struct](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct) used to maintaininformation needed by
*Resource Timing* and *Navigation Timing*. It has thefollowing [items](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item): [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing") [[NAVIGATION-TIMING]](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing")
*encoded size* (default 0)     *decoded size* (default 0)     A number.   
*content type* (default the empty string)     An [ASCII string](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string).    
*content encoding* (default the empty string)     An [ASCII string](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string).    


To*create an opaque timing info*,given a [fetch timing info](#fetch-timing-info) timingInfo,
return a new[fetch timing info](#fetch-timing-info) whose [start time](#fetch-timing-info-start-time) and[post-redirect start time](#fetch-timing-info-post-redirect-start-time) are
timingInfo’s[start time](#fetch-timing-info-start-time), [end time](#fetch-timing-info-end-time)
istimingInfo’s [end time](#fetch-timing-info-end-time), and[render-blocking](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking) is
timingInfo’s[render-blocking](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking).


To *queue a fetch task*, given an algorithm algorithm, a[global object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object) or a [parallel queue](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue)
taskDestination, run thesesteps:


1.       If taskDestination is a [parallel queue](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue), then [enqueue](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps) algorithm to taskDestination.

2.       Otherwise, [queue a global task](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task) on the [networking task source](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#networking-task-source) with taskDestination and algorithm.


To check if the [environment settings object](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object)
environment*is offline*:

-       If the user agent assumes it does not have internet connectivity, then return true.

-       Return environment’s [WebDriver BiDi network is offline](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline).


---

To *serialize an integer*, represent it as a string of the shortest possible decimalnumber.

This will be replaced by a more descriptive algorithm in Infra. See[infra/201](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201).

### 2.1. URL

A *local scheme* is "`about`", "`blob`", or"`data`".

A [URL](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url) *is local* if its [scheme](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme) is a[local scheme](#local-scheme).

This definition is also used by *Referrer Policy*. [[REFERRER]](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy")

An *HTTP(S) scheme* is "`http`" or"`https`".

A *fetch scheme* is "`about`", "`blob`","`data`", "`file`", or an [HTTP(S) scheme](#http-scheme).

[HTTP(S) scheme](#http-scheme) and [fetch scheme](#fetch-scheme) are also used by *HTML*.[[HTML]](#biblio-html "HTML Standard")

### 2.2. HTTP

While [fetching](#concept-fetch) encompasses more than just HTTP, itborrows a number of concepts from HTTP and applies these to resources obtained via othermeans (e.g., `data`
URLs).

An *HTTP tab or space* is U+0009 TAB or U+0020 SPACE.

*HTTP whitespace* is U+000A LF, U+000D CR, or an [HTTP tab or space](#http-tab-or-space).

[HTTP whitespace](#http-whitespace) is only useful for specific constructs that are reused outsidethe context of HTTP headers (e.g., [MIME types](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type)). For HTTP header values, using[HTTP tab or space](#http-tab-or-space) is preferred, and outside that context [ASCII whitespace](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace) ispreferred. Unlike [ASCII whitespace](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace) this excludes U+000C FF.

An *HTTP newline byte* is 0x0A (LF) or 0x0D (CR).

An *HTTP tab or space byte* is 0x09 (HT) or 0x20 (SP).

An *HTTP whitespace byte* is an [HTTP newline byte](#http-newline-byte) or[HTTP tab or space byte](#http-tab-or-space-byte).


To*collect an HTTP quoted string*from a [string](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string) input,
given a [position variable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable) positionand
an optional boolean extract-value (default false):


1.       Let positionStart be position.

2.       Let value be the empty string.

3.       [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert): the [code point](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point) at position within input is U+0022 (").

4.       Advance position by 1.

5.       While true:  


1.         Append the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points) that are not U+0022 (")   or U+005C (\\) from input, given position, to value.   

2.         If position is past the end of input, then   [break](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break).   

3.         Let quoteOrBackslash be the [code point](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point) at position within   input.   

4.         Advance position by 1.   

5.         If quoteOrBackslash is U+005C (\\), then:    


1.           If position is past the end of input, then append U+005C (\\) to     value and [break](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break).     

2.           Append the [code point](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point) at position within input to     value.     

3.           Advance position by 1.    


-         Otherwise:    


1.           [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert): quoteOrBackslash is U+0022 (").     

2.           [Break](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break).    


-       If extract-value is true, then return value.

-       Return the [code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point) from positionStart to position, inclusive, within input.


|   |
||------------------------|------------------------|---------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| - | -                      | -                      | -                                     |
|   | "`"\`"                 | "`"\`"                 | "`\`"                                 | 2                                                                                        |
|   | "`"Hello" World`"      | "`"Hello"`"            | "`Hello`"                             | 7                                                                                        |
|   | "`"Hello \\ World\""`" | "`"Hello \\ World\""`" | "`Hello \ World"`"                    | 18                                                                                       |

The [position variable](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") always starts at 0 in these examples.

#### 2.2.1. Methods

A *method* is a [byte sequence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that matches the[method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") token production.

A *CORS-safelisted method* is a[method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is \``GET`\`,\``HEAD`\`,
or \``POST`\`.

A *forbidden method* is a [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is a[byte-case-insensitive](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") match for
\``CONNECT`\`,\``TRACE`\`, or \``TRACK`\`.[[HTTPVERBSEC1]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), [[HTTPVERBSEC2]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), [[HTTPVERBSEC3]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

To *normalize* a[method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), if it is a [byte-case-insensitive](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")match for
\``DELETE`\`, \``GET`\`,\``HEAD`\`, \``OPTIONS`\`, \``POST`\`, or\``PUT`\`, [byte-uppercase](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") it.

[Normalization](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is done for backwards compatibility andconsistency across APIs as [methods](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") are actually "case-sensitive".

Using \``patch`\` is highly likely to result in a\``405 Method Not Allowed`\`. \``PATCH`\` is much more likely tosucceed.

There are no restrictions on [methods](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). \``CHICKEN`\` is
perfectlyacceptable (and not a misspelling of \``CHECKIN`\`). Other than those that are[normalized](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") there are no casing restrictions
either.\``Egg`\` or \``eGg`\` would be fine, though uppercase is encouraged forconsistency.

#### 2.2.2. Headers

HTTP generally refers to a header as a "field" or "header field". The web platformuses
the more colloquial term "header". [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A *header list* is a [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of zero or more[headers](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). It is initially « ».

A [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is essentially a specialized multimap: an ordered list ofkey-value pairs with potentially duplicate keys. Since headers other than
\``Set-Cookie`\`are always combined when exposed to client-side JavaScript, implementations
could choose a moreefficient representation, as long as they also support an associated
data structure for\``Set-Cookie`\` headers.


To*get a structured field value*given a [header name](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") name
and a string type from a[header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") list, run these
steps. They return null or a[structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").


1.       [Assert](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"): type is one of "`dictionary`", "`list`", or "`item`".

2.       Let value be the result of [getting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") name from list.

3.       If value is null, then return null.

4.       Let result be the result of [parsing structured fields](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with input_string set to value and header_type set to type.

5.       If parsing failed, then return null.

6.       Return result.


[Get a structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") intentionally does not distinguish between a[header](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") not being present and its [value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") failing to parse as a[structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). This ensures uniform processing across the web platform.


To*set a structured field value*given a [tuple](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") ([header name](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
name, [structured field value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")structuredValue),
in a [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") list:


1.       Let serializedValue be the result of executing the [serializing structured fields](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") algorithm on structuredValue.

2.       [Set](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") (name, serializedValue) in list.


[Structured field values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") are defined as objects which HTTP can (eventually)serialize in interesting and efficient ways. For the moment, Fetch only supports[header values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") as [byte sequences](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), which means that these objects can be set in[header lists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") only via serialization, and they can be obtained from[header lists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") only by parsing. In the future the fact that they are objects might bepreserved end-to-end. [[RFC9651]](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle")

---


A [header list](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "dt-updated") list*contains* a[header name](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues "You can also press the 'y' key")
name if list [contains](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key") a[header](/commit-snapshots/394d20d144ed1401c2c0e02c35bc3608cb2a2269/ "You can also press the 'y' key") whose [name](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") is a [byte-case-insensitive](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese") match
forname.


To *get* a [header name](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean")name from a [header list](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean")
list, run these steps. They return nullor a [header value](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3").


1.       If list [does not contain](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") name, then return null.

2.       Return the [values](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") of all [headers](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") in list whose [name](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a [byte-case-insensitive](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for name, separated from each other by 0x2C 0x20, in order.


To*get, decode, and split*a [header name](#bodies "Content Security Policy Level 3") name
from [header list](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") list, run thesesteps.
They return null or a [list](#responses "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [strings](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3").


1.       Let value be the result of [getting](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") name from list.

2.       If value is null, then return null.

3.       Return the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3") value.


This is how [get, decode, and split](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3") functions in practice with
\``A`\` as the name argument:

|                        |
||----------------------------------|--------|
| -                      | -                                |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: nosniff,            |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`nosniff`", "" »            |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: nosniff             |
| B: sniff               |
| A:                     |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A:                     |
| B: sniff               |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "" »                         |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| B: sniff               |
| ```                    |
|                        | null                             |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: text/html;", x/x``` |
|                        | « "`text/html;", x/x`" »       |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: text/html;"         |
| A: x/x                 |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: x/x;test="hi",y/y   |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`x/x;test="hi"`", "`y/y`" » |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: x/x;test="hi"       |
| C: **bingo**           |
| A: y/y                 |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: x / x,,,1           |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`x / x`", "", "", "`1`" »   |
|                        |                                  |
| ```content             |
| A: x / x               |
| A:,                   |
| A: 1                   |
| ```                    |
|                        |
|                        |                                  |
| ```content             |
| A: "1,2", 3            |
| ```                    |
|                        | « "`"1,2"`", "`3`" »           |
|                        |                                  |
| ```secno               |
| A: "1,2"               |
| D: 4                   |
| A: 3                   |
| ```                    |
|                        |


To*get, decode, and split*a [header value](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")
value, run these steps. They return a [list](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") of[strings](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3").


1.       Let input be the result of [isomorphic decoding](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") value.

2.       Let position be a [position variable](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") for input, initially pointing at the start of input.

3.       Let values be a [list](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [strings](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3"), initially « ».

4.       Let temporaryValue be the empty string.

5.       While true:  


1.         Append the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") that are not U+0022 (") or    U+002C (,) from input, given position, to temporaryValue.    

	The result might be the empty string.   

2.         If position is not past the end of input and the    [code point](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") at position within input is U+0022 ("):    


1.           Append the result of [collecting an HTTP quoted string](#cors-preflight-cache "Content Security Policy Level 3") from input,     given position, to temporaryValue.     

2. If position is not past the end of input, then     [continue](#tao-check "Content Security Policy Level 3").            


-         Remove all [HTTP tab or space](#deferred-fetch-quota "Content Security Policy Level 3") from the start and end of temporaryValue.   

-         [Append](#fetch-api "Content Security Policy Level 3") temporaryValue to values.   

-         Set temporaryValue to the empty string.   

-         If position is past the end of input, then return values.   

-         [Assert](#body-mixin "Content Security Policy Level 3"): the [code point](#request-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") at position within   input is U+002C (,).   

-         Advance position by 1.  


Except for blessed call sites, the algorithm directly above is not to be invokeddirectly.
Use [get, decode, and split](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3") instead.


To *append* a [header](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3")(name,
value) to a [header list](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3") list:


1.       If list [contains](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level 3") name, then set name  to the first such [header](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [name](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3").  

	This reuses the casing of the [name](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level 3") of the [header](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")  already in list, if any. If there are multiple matched [headers](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") their  [names](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3") will all be identical.

2.       [Append](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3") (name, value) to list.


To *delete* a[header name](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") name from
a [header list](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") list,[remove](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") all [headers](#informative "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose [name](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a[byte-case-insensitive](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for
name from list.


To *set* a [header](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Content Security Policy Level 3")(name,
value) in a [header list](#biblio-sw "Fetch Metadata Request Headers") list:


1.       If list [contains](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content") name, then set the [value](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests") of the first such [header](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy") to value and [remove](#http-origin "Referrer Policy") the others.

2.       Otherwise, [append](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") (name, value) to list.


To *combine* a[header](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") (name, value) in a [header list](#dom-global-fetch "Infra
Standard")list:


1.       If list [contains](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") name, then set the [value](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") of the first such [header](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") to its [value](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard"), followed by 0x2C 0x20, followed by value.

2.       Otherwise, [append](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") (name, value) to list.


[Combine](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") is used by `[XMLHttpRequest](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF")`
and the[WebSocket protocol handshake](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF").


To *convert header names to a sorted-lowercase set*, given a [list](#biblio-mimesniff "MIME Sniffing Standard") of[names](#biblio-streams "Streams Standard")
headerNames, run these steps. They return an[ordered set](#biblio-webidl "Web IDL Standard") of [header names](#biblio-websockets "WebSockets Standard").


1.       Let headerNamesSet be a new [ordered set](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF").

2.       [For each](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") name of headerNames, [append](#authentication-entry "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") the result of [byte-lowercasing](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") name to headerNamesSet.

3.       Return the result of [sorting](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") headerNamesSet in ascending order with [byte less than](#biblio-tls "The Transport Layer Security (TLS) Protocol Version 1.3").


To *sort and combine* a[header list](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") list, run
these steps. They return a [header list](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing").


1.       Let headers be a [header list](#concept-request "Resource Timing").

2.       Let names be the result of [convert header names to a sorted-lowercase set](#concept-request "Resource Timing") with all the [names](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") of the [headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") in list.

3.       [For each](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") name of names:  


1.         If name is \``set-cookie`\`, then:    


1.           Let values be a list of all [values](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") of     [headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") in list whose [name](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is a     [byte-case-insensitive](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") match for name, in order.     

2.           [For each](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") value of values:      


1.             [Append](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") (name, value) to headers.      


-         Otherwise:    


1.           Let value be the result of [getting](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") name     from list.     

2.           [Assert](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource Timing"): value is non-null.     

3.           [Append](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") (name, value) to headers.    


-       Return headers.


---

A *header* is a [tuple](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") that consists of a*name*
(a [header name](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")) and*value* (a [header value](#concept-response "Resource Timing")).

A *header name* is a [byte sequence](#concept-response "Resource Timing") that matches the[field-name](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") token production.

A *header value* is a [byte sequence](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") that matches the followingconditions:

-      Has no leading or trailing [HTTP tab or space bytes](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").

-      Contains no 0x00 (NUL) or [HTTP newline bytes](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").


The definition of [header value](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is not defined in terms of the[field-value](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") token production as it is[not compatible with deployed content](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").


To *normalize* a[byte sequence](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
potentialValue, remove any leading and trailing[HTTP whitespace bytes](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") from
potentialValue.

---


To determine whether a [header](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")
(name, value)is a *CORS-safelisted request-header*, run these steps:


1.       If value’s [length](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is greater than 128, then return false.

2.       [Byte-lowercase](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") name and switch on the result:  
\``accept`\`                  If value contains a [CORS-unsafe request-header byte](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), then return false.   
\``accept-language`\`          \``content-language`\`                  If value contains a byte that is not in the range 0x30 (0) to 0x39 (9),   inclusive, is not in the range 0x41 (A) to 0x5A (Z), inclusive, is not in the range 0x61 (a) to   0x7A (z), inclusive, and is not 0x20 (SP), 0x2A (\*), 0x2C (,), 0x2D (-), 0x2E (.), 0x3B (;), or   0x3D (=), then return false.   
\``content-type`\`                 

1.           If value contains a [CORS-unsafe request-header byte](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing"), then return     false.     

2.           Let mimeType be the result of [parsing](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") the     result of [isomorphic decoding](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") value.     

3.           If mimeType is failure, then return false.     

4.           If mimeType’s [essence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") is not     "`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`", "`multipart/form-data`", or     "`text/plain`", then return false.    


This intentionally does not use [extract a MIME type](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") as that algorithm is    rather forgiving and servers are not expected to implement it.    


If [extract a MIME type](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") were used the following request would not result in a CORS     preflight and a naïve parser on the server might treat the request body as JSON:     

		fetch("https://victim.example/naïve-endpoint", {
method: "POST",
headers: [
["Content-Type", "application/json"],
["Content-Type", "text/plain"]
],
credentials: "include",
body: JSON.stringify(exerciseForTheReader)
});
\``range`\`                 

1.           Let rangeValue be the result of [parsing a single range header value](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")     given value and false.     

2.           If rangeValue is failure, then return false.     

3.           If rangeValue[0] is null, then return false.      

	As web browsers have historically not emitted ranges such as      \``bytes=-500`\` this algorithm does not safelist them.    


Otherwise                  Return false.  

-       Return true.


There are limited exceptions to the \``Content-Type`\` header safelist, asdocumented in [CORS protocol exceptions](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing").


A *CORS-unsafe request-header byte* is a byte byte for which one of thefollowing
is true:

-       byte is less than 0x20 and is not 0x09 HT

-       byte is 0x22 ("), 0x28 (left parenthesis), 0x29 (right parenthesis), 0x3A (:), 0x3C (<), 0x3E (>), 0x3F (?), 0x40 (@), 0x5B ([), 0x5C (\\), 0x5D (]), 0x7B ({), 0x7D (}), or 0x7F DEL.


The *CORS-unsafe request-header names*, given a [header list](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource
Timing")headers, are determined as follows:


1.       Let unsafeNames be a new [list](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing").

2.       Let potentiallyUnsafeNames be a new [list](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing").

3.       Let safelistValueSize be 0.

4.       [For each](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") header of headers:  


1.         If header is not a [CORS-safelisted request-header](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing"), then   [append](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") header’s [name](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to unsafeNames.   

2.         Otherwise, [append](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") header’s [name](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to   potentiallyUnsafeNames and increase safelistValueSize by   header’s [value](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")’s [length](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing").  


-       If safelistValueSize is greater than 1024, then [for each](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") name of potentiallyUnsafeNames, [append](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") name to unsafeNames.

-       Return the result of [convert header names to a sorted-lowercase set](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") with unsafeNames.


A *CORS non-wildcard request-header name* is a [header name](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") that is a[byte-case-insensitive](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") match for
\``Authorization`\`.

A *privileged no-CORS request-header name* is a [header name](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") that isa [byte-case-insensitive](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") match for one of

- \``Range`\`.   


These are headers that can be set by privileged APIs, and will be preserved if their
associated request object is copied, but will be removed if the request is modified
by unprivileged APIs.

\``Range`\` headers are commonly used by [downloads](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") and [media fetches](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

A helper is provided to [add a range header](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") to a particular request.

A *CORS-safelisted response-header name*, given a [list](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") of[header names](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")
list, is a [header name](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") that is a[byte-case-insensitive](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") match for one of

- \``Cache-Control`\`     
- \``Content-Language`\`     
- \``Content-Length`\`     
- \``Content-Type`\`     
- \``Expires`\`     
- \``Last-Modified`\`     
- \``Pragma`\`     
- Any [item](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") in list that is not a [forbidden response-header name](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").   

A *no-CORS-safelisted request-header name* is a [header name](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") thatis a [byte-case-insensitive](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") match for one of

- \``Accept`\`     
- \``Accept-Language`\`     
- \``Content-Language`\`     
- \``Content-Type`\`   


To determine whether a [header](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") (name,
value) is a*no-CORS-safelisted request-header*, run these steps:


1.       If name is not a [no-CORS-safelisted request-header name](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing"), then return false.

2.       Return whether (name, value) is a [CORS-safelisted request-header](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing").


A [header](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") (name, value)
is*forbidden request-header* if these steps return true:


1.       If name is a [byte-case-insensitive](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") match for one of:  
2. \``Accept-Charset`\`          
3. \``Accept-Encoding`\`          
4. \`[`Access-Control-Request-Headers`](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing")\`          
5. \`[`Access-Control-Request-Method`](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing")\`          
6. \``Connection`\`          
7. \``Content-Length`\`          
8. \``Cookie`\`          
9. \``Cookie2`\`          
10. \``Date`\`          
11. \``DNT`\`          
12. \``Expect`\`          
13. \``Host`\`          
14. \``Keep-Alive`\`          
15. \`[`Origin`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")\`          
16. \``Referer`\`          
17. \``Set-Cookie`\`          
18. \``TE`\`          
19. \``Trailer`\`          
20. \``Transfer-Encoding`\`          
21. \``Upgrade`\`          
22. \``Via`\`        

then return true.

23.       
If name when [byte-lowercased](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") [starts with](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")
\``proxy-`\` or \``sec-`\`, then return true.

24.       
If name is a [byte-case-insensitive](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") match for one of:  

25. \``X-HTTP-Method`\`          
26. \``X-HTTP-Method-Override`\`          
27. \``X-Method-Override`\`        

then:  


1.         Let parsedValues be the result of   [getting, decoding, and splitting](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") value.   

2.         [For each](#fetch-timing-info-post-redirect-start-time "Referrer Policy") method of parsedValues: if the   [isomorphic encoding](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") of method is a [forbidden method](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy"), then return true.  


-       
Return false.


These are forbidden so the user agent remains in full control over them.

[Header names](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy") starting with \``Sec-`\`
are reserved to allow new [headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") to be minted that are safe from APIs using [fetch](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") that allow control over [headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") by developers, such as `[XMLHttpRequest](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer
Policy")`. [[XHR]](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy")

The \``Set-Cookie`\` header is semantically a response header, so it is not useful on requests.
Because \``Set-Cookie`\` headers cannot be combined, they require more complex handling in
the `[Headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy")`
object. It is forbidden here to avoid leaking this complexity into requests.

A *forbidden response-header name* is a [header name](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#networking-task-source "Referrer Policy") that is a[byte-case-insensitive](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#networking-task-source "Referrer Policy") match for one of:

- \``Set-Cookie`\`     
- \``Set-Cookie2`\`   

A *request-body-header name* is a [header name](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") that is a[byte-case-insensitive](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") match for one of:

- \``Content-Encoding`\`     
- \``Content-Language`\`     
- \``Content-Location`\`     
- \``Content-Type`\`   

---


To *extract header values*given a [header](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy")
header, run these steps:


1.       If parsing header’s [value](#url "Referrer Policy"), per the [ABNF](#url "Referrer Policy") for header’s [name](#url "Referrer Policy"), fails, then return failure.

2.       Return one or more [values](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy") resulting from parsing header’s [value](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy"), per the [ABNF](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy") for header’s [name](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy").


To*extract header list values*given a [header name](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") name
and a [header list](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") list,run these steps:


1.       If list [does not contain](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") name, then return null.

2.       If the [ABNF](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") for name allows a single [header](#fetch-scheme "HTML Standard") and list  [contains](#biblio-html "HTML Standard") more than one, then return failure.  

	If different error handling is needed, extract the desired [header](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")  first.

3.       Let values be an empty [list](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

4.       For each [header](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") header list  [contains](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose [name](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is name:  


1.         Let extract be the result of [extracting header values](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") from   header.   

2.         If extract is failure, then return failure.   

3.         Append each [value](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") in extract, in order, to values.  


-       Return values.


To *build a content range* given an integer rangeStart, an integerrangeEnd, and an
integer fullLength, run these steps:


1.       Let contentRange be \``bytes `\`.

2.       Append rangeStart, [serialized](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and [isomorphic encoded](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), to contentRange.

3.       Append 0x2D (-) to contentRange.

4.       Append rangeEnd, [serialized](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and [isomorphic encoded](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to contentRange.

5.       Append 0x2F (/) to contentRange.

6.       Append fullLength, [serialized](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and [isomorphic encoded](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to contentRange.

7.       Return contentRange.


To *parse a single range header value* from a[byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")
value and a boolean allowWhitespace, run these steps:


1.       Let data be the [isomorphic decoding](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") of value.

2.       If data does not [start with](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") "`bytes`", then return failure.

3.       Let position be a [position variable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") for data, initially pointing at the 5th [code point](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") of data.

4.       If allowWhitespace is true, [collect a sequence of code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") that are [HTTP tab or space](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), from data given position.

5.       If the [code point](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") at position within data is not U+003D (=), then return failure.

6.       Advance position by 1.

7.       If allowWhitespace is true, [collect a sequence of code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") that are [HTTP tab or space](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), from data given position.

8.       Let rangeStart be the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") that are [ASCII digits](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), from data given position.

9.       Let rangeStartValue be rangeStart, interpreted as decimal number, if rangeStart is not the empty string; otherwise null.

10.       If allowWhitespace is true, [collect a sequence of code points](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") that are [HTTP tab or space](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), from data given position.

11.       If the [code point](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") at position within data is not U+002D (-), then return failure.

12.       Advance position by 1.

13.       If allowWhitespace is true, [collect a sequence of code points](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") that are [HTTP tab or space](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css), from data given position.

14.       Let rangeEnd be the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) that are [ASCII digits](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"), from data given position.

15.       Let rangeEndValue be rangeEnd, interpreted as decimal number, if rangeEnd is not the empty string; otherwise null.

16.       If position is not past the end of data, then return failure.

17.       If rangeEndValue and rangeStartValue are null, then return failure.

18.       If rangeStartValue and rangeEndValue are numbers, and rangeStartValue is greater than rangeEndValue, then return failure.

19.       Return (rangeStartValue, rangeEndValue).  

	The range end or start can be omitted, e.g., \``bytes=0-`\` or  \``bytes=-500`\` are valid ranges.


[Parse a single range header value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") succeeds for a subset of allowed range headervalues, but it is the most common form used by user agents when requesting media or resumingdownloads. This format of range header value can be set using [add a range header](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

---

A *default \``User-Agent`\` value* is an[implementation-defined](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [header value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for the
\``User-Agent`\`[header](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

For unfortunate web compatibility reasons, web browsers are strongly encouraged tohave
this value start with \``Mozilla/5.0 (`\` and be generally modeled after other webbrowsers.


To get the [environment settings object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") environment’s*environment
default \``User-Agent`\` value*:


1.       Let userAgent be the [WebDriver BiDi emulated User-Agent](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for environment.

2.       If userAgent is non-null, then return userAgent, [isomorphic encoded](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

3.       Return the [default \``User-Agent`\` value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").


The *document \``Accept`\` header value* is\``text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8`\`.

#### 2.2.3. Statuses

A *status* is an integer in the range 0 to 999, inclusive.

Various edge cases in mapping HTTP/1’s `status-code` to this concept areworked on in [issue #1156](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

A *null body status* is a [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is 101, 103, 204, 205, or 304.

An *ok status* is a [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") in the range 200 to 299, inclusive.

A *range status* is a [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is 206 or 416.

A *redirect status* is a [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is 301, 302, 303, 307, or 308.

#### 2.2.4. Bodies

A *body* consists of:

-      A *stream* (a `[ReadableStream](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` object).

-      A *source* (null, a [byte sequence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), a `[Blob](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` object, or a `[FormData](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` object), initially null.

-      A *length* (null or an integer), initially null.


To *clone* a[body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") body, run these steps:


1.       Let « out1, out2 » be the result of [teeing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") body’s [stream](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

2.       Set body’s [stream](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to out1.

3.       Return a [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose [stream](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is out2 and other members are copied from body.


To get a [byte sequence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") bytes*as a body*, return
the [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of theresult of [safely extracting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
bytes.

---


To *incrementally read* a [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") body, given analgorithm
processBodyChunk, an algorithm processEndOfBody, an algorithmprocessBodyError, and
an optional null, [parallel queue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), or[global object](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle")
taskDestination (default null), run these steps.processBodyChunk must be an algorithm
accepting a [byte sequence](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y'
key").processEndOfBody must be an algorithm accepting no arguments. processBodyErrormust
be an algorithm accepting an exception.


1.       If taskDestination is null, then set taskDestination to the result of [starting a new parallel queue](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key").

2.       Let reader be the result of [getting a reader](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese") for  body’s [stream](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese").  

	This operation will not throw an exception.

3.       Perform the [incrementally-read loop](ja "Simplified Chinese") given reader, taskDestination, processBodyChunk, processEndOfBody, and processBodyError.


To perform the *incrementally-read loop*, given a `[ReadableStreamDefaultReader](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3")`
objectreader, [parallel queue](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") or [global object](#preface "Content Security Policy Level
3")taskDestination, algorithm processBodyChunk, algorithmprocessEndOfBody, and algorithm
processBodyError:


1.       Let readRequest be the following [read request](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3"):  
[chunk steps](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3"), given chunk                 

1.           Let continueAlgorithm be null.     

2.           If chunk is not a `[Uint8Array](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object, then set     continueAlgorithm to this step: run processBodyError given a     `[TypeError](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3")`.     

3.           Otherwise:      


1.             Let bytes be a [copy of](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3")        chunk.        

	Implementations are strongly encouraged to use an implementation strategy that        avoids this copy where possible.       

2.             Set continueAlgorithm to these steps:        


1.               Run processBodyChunk given bytes.         

2.               Perform the [incrementally-read loop](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") given reader,         taskDestination, processBodyChunk, processEndOfBody, and         processBodyError.        


-           [Queue a fetch task](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3") given continueAlgorithm and     taskDestination.    


[close steps](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")                 

1.           [Queue a fetch task](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") given processEndOfBody and   taskDestination.


[error steps](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), given e                 

1.           [Queue a fetch task](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") to run processBodyError given e,   with taskDestination.


-       [Read a chunk](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3") from reader given readRequest.


To *fully read* a [body](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") body,
given an algorithmprocessBody, an algorithm processBodyError, and an optional null,[parallel queue](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), or [global object](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")
taskDestination (defaultnull), run these steps. processBody must be an algorithm
accepting a[byte sequence](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3").
processBodyError must be an algorithm optionally accepting an[exception](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3").


1.       If taskDestination is null, then set taskDestination to the result of [starting a new parallel queue](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3").

2.       Let successSteps given a [byte sequence](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3") bytes be to [queue a fetch task](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") to run processBody given bytes, with taskDestination.

3.       Let errorSteps optionally given an [exception](#http-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") exception be to [queue a fetch task](#http-redirect-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") to run processBodyError given exception, with taskDestination.

4.       Let reader be the result of [getting a reader](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") for body’s [stream](#cors-preflight-cache "Content Security Policy Level 3"). If that threw an exception, then run errorSteps with that exception and return.

5.       [Read all bytes](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") from reader, given successSteps and errorSteps.


---

A *body with type* is a [tuple](#fetch-api "Content Security Policy Level 3") that consists of
a*body* (a [body](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3")) and a*type*
(a [header value](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") or null).

---


To *handle content codings* given codings and bytes, runthese steps:


1.       If codings are not supported, then return bytes.

2.       Return the result of decoding bytes with codings as explained in HTTP, if decoding does not result in an error, and failure otherwise. [[HTTP]](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")


#### 2.2.5. Requests

This section documents how requests work in detail. To get started, see[Setting up a request](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level 3").

The input to [fetch](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a*request*.

A [request](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*method*
(a[method](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3")). Unless stated otherwise it
is\``GET`\`.

This can be updated during redirects to \``GET`\` as described in[HTTP fetch](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3").

A [request](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated *URL*(a [URL](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3")).

Implementations are encouraged to make this a pointer to the first [URL](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") in[request](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [URL list](#informative "Content Security Policy Level 3"). It is provided as a distinct field solely forthe convenience of other standards hooking into Fetch.

A [request](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*local-URLs-only
flag*. Unless stated otherwise it isunset.

A [request](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*header
list* (a[header list](#biblio-sw "Fetch Metadata Request Headers")). Unless stated otherwise it is « ».

A [request](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content") has an associated*unsafe-request flag*.
Unless stated otherwise itis unset.

The [unsafe-request flag](#http-origin "Referrer Policy") is set by APIs such as[`fetch()`](#http-origin "The Web Origin Concept")  and `[XMLHttpRequest](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept")`
to ensure a [CORS-preflight fetch](#preface "Infra Standard")is done based on the supplied [method](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") and [header list](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard"). It doesnot free an API from outlawing [forbidden methods](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") and [forbidden request-headers](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard").

A [request](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") has an associated*body* (null, a
[byte sequence](#infrastructure "Infra Standard"), or a[body](#infrastructure "Infra Standard")). Unless stated otherwise it is null.

A [byte sequence](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") will be [safely extracted](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard") into a[body](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") early on in [fetch](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF"). As part of [HTTP fetch](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") it is possible forthis field to be set to null due to certain redirects.

---

A [request](#biblio-html "Encoding Standard") has an associated*client* (null or
an[environment settings object](#biblio-streams "Streams Standard")).

A [request](#biblio-websockets "Web IDL Standard") has an associated*reserved client*(null
or an [environment](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF")). Unless stated otherwise it is null.

This is only used by [navigation requests](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") and worker requests, but not serviceworker requests.

A [request](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") has an
associated*replaces client id*(a string). Unless stated otherwise it is the empty
string.

This is only used by [navigation requests](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics"). It is the [id](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")of the [target browsing context](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [active document](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")’s[environment settings object](#concept-request "Resource Timing").

A [request](#concept-request "Resource Timing") has an associated*traversable for
user prompts*, that is"`no-traversable`", "`client`", or a [traversable navigable](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").Unless stated otherwise it is
"`client`".


This is used to determine whether and where to show necessary UI for the request,
such as authentication prompts or client certificate dialogs.
"`no-traversable`"       No UI is shown; usually the request fails with a [network error](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").      
"`client`"       This value will automatically be changed to either "`no-traversable`" or to a  [traversable navigable](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") derived from the request’s [client](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") during  [fetching](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"). This provides a convenient way for standards to not have to  explicitly set a request’s [traversable for user prompts](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").      
a [traversable navigable](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")      
The UI shown will be associated with the browser interface elements that are displaying
that  [traversable navigable](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource Timing").      

When displaying a user interface associated with a request in that request’s[traversable for user prompts](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), the user agent should update the address bar todisplay something derived from the request’s [current URL](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") (and not, e.g., leaveit at its previous value, derived from the URL of the request’s initiator). Additionally, the useragent should avoid displaying content from the request’s initiator in the[traversable for user prompts](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), especially in the case of cross-origin requests.Displaying a blank page behind such prompts is a good way to fulfill these requirements. Failing tofollow these guidelines can confuse users as to which origin is responsible for the prompt.

A [request](#concept-response "Resource Timing") has an associated boolean*keepalive*.
Unless stated otherwise it isfalse.

This can be used to allow the request to outlive the[environment settings object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), e.g., `navigator.sendBeacon()`
and the HTML`img` element use this. Requests with this set to true are subject to additionalprocessing
requirements.

A [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") has an
associated*initiator type*, which is
null,"`audio`","`beacon`","`body`","`css`","`early-hints`","`embed`","`fetch`","`font`","`frame`","`iframe`","`image`","`img`","`input`","`link`","`object`","`ping`","`script`","`track`","`video`","`xmlhttprequest`",
or"`other`". Unless stated otherwise it is null. [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")

A [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") has an associated
*service-workers mode*, thatis "`all`" or "`none`". Unless stated otherwise it is "`all`".


This determines which service workers will receive a `[fetch](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")`
event for this fetch.
"`all`"       Relevant service workers will get a `[fetch](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")`
event for this fetch.       "`none`"       No service workers will get events for this
fetch.           
A [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*initiator*, which
isthe empty string,"`download`","`imageset`","`manifest`","`prefetch`","`prerender`", or"`xslt`". Unless stated otherwise it is
the empty string.

A [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s [initiator](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") is not particularly granular forthe time being as other specifications do not require it to be. It is primarily a specificationdevice to assist defining CSP and Mixed Content. It is not exposed to JavaScript. [[CSP]](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") [[MIX]](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing")

A *destination type* is one of:the empty
string,"`audio`","`audioworklet`","`document`","`embed`","`font`","`frame`","`iframe`","`image`","`json`","`manifest`","`object`","`paintworklet`","`report`","`script`","`serviceworker`","`sharedworker`","`style`","`text`","`track`","`video`","`webidentity`","`worker`",
or"`xslt`".

A [request](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") has an
associated*destination*, which is[destination type](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing"). Unless stated otherwise it is the empty string.

These are reflected on `[RequestDestination](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")` except for "`serviceworker`"and
"`webidentity`" as fetches with those destinations skip service workers.

A [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [destination](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")
is*script-like* if it is "`audioworklet`","`paintworklet`", "`script`", "`serviceworker`","`sharedworker`", or "`worker`".

Algorithms that use [script-like](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") should also consider"`xslt`"
as that too can cause script execution. It is not included in the list as it isnot
always relevant and might require different behavior.

It is not exhaustive with respect to features. Features need to have the relevant values defined in their respective standards.

|   | "`worker`"                                                                                                     | `child-src`, `script-src`, `worker-src`                                                                          | `Worker`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|   | "`json`"                                                                                                       | `connect-src`                                                                                                    | `import "..." with { type: "json" }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|   | "`style`"                                                                                                      | `style-src`                                                                                                      | HTML’s `<link rel=stylesheet>`, CSS' `@import`, `import "..." with { type: "css" }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|   | "`text`"                                                                                                       | `connect-src`                                                                                                    | `import "..." with { type: "text" }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|   | "`track`"                                                                                                      | `media-src`                                                                                                      | HTML’s `<track>`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|   | "`video`"                                                                                                      | `media-src`                                                                                                      | HTML’s `<video>` element                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|   | "`download`"                                                                                                   | ""                                                                                                               | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | HTML’s `download=""`, "Save Link As…" UI        |
|   | "`imageset`"                                                                                                   | "`image`"                                                                                                        | `img-src`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | HTML’s `<img srcset>` and `<picture>` |
