|   | "`manifest`"                                                                                                   | "`manifest`"                                                                                                     | `manifest-src`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | HTML’s `<link rel=manifest>`                |
|   | "`prefetch`"                                                                                                   | ""                                                                                                               | `default-src` (no specific directive)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | HTML’s `<link rel=prefetch>`                |
|   | "`prerender`"                                                                                                  | HTML’s `<link rel=prerender>`                                                                            |
|   | "`xslt`"                                                                                                       | "`xslt`"                                                                                                         | `script-src`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `<?xml-stylesheet>`                           |

CSP’s `form-action` needs to be a hook directly in HTML’s navigate or form submission algorithm.


CSP will also need to check [request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [client](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [global object](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [associated `Document`](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [ancestor navigables](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") for various CSP directives.

---

A [request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") has an
associated*priority*, which is "`high`", "`low`", or"`auto`". Unless stated otherwise it is
"`auto`".

A [request](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") has an associated*internal
priority* (null or an[implementation-defined](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") object). Unless otherwise stated it is null.

A [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") has an associated*origin*,
which is"`client`" or an [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing"). Unless stated otherwise it
is"`client`".

"`client`" is changed to an [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") during[fetching](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"). It provides a convenient way for standards to not have to set[request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing").

A [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") has an
associated*top-level navigation initiator origin*, which is an [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Navigation Timing")or null. Unless stated otherwise it is null.

A [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") has an associated*policy
container*, which is"`client`" or a [policy container](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"). Unless stated otherwise it
is"`client`".

"`client`" is changed to a [policy container](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") during[fetching](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"). It provides a convenient way for standards to not have to set[request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s [policy container](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy").

A [request](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") has an associated*referrer*, which
is"`no-referrer`", "`client`", or a [URL](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy"). Unless stated otherwise itis "`client`".

"`client`" is changed to "`no-referrer`" or a [URL](#fetch-timing-info-post-redirect-start-time "Referrer Policy")during [fetching](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy"). It provides a convenient way for standards to not have to set[request](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy")’s [referrer](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy").

A [request](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy") has an associated*referrer
policy*, which is a[referrer policy](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy"). Unless stated otherwise it is the empty string. [[REFERRER]](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy")

This can be used to override the referrer policy to be used for this[request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy").

A [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy") has an
associated*mode*, which is"`same-origin`", "`cors`", "`no-cors`","`navigate`", "`websocket`", or "`webtransport`".Unless stated otherwise,
it is "`no-cors`".


"`same-origin`"       Used to ensure requests are made to same-origin URLs. [Fetch](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") will return a  [network error](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") if the request is not made to a same-origin URL.      
"`cors`"       For requests whose [response tainting](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") gets set to
"`cors`", makes  the request a [CORS request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") — in which case, fetch will return a [network error](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") if the  requested resource does not understand the [CORS protocol](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy"), or if the requested resource is  one that intentionally does not participate in the [CORS protocol](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy").      
"`no-cors`"       Restricts requests to using [CORS-safelisted methods](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") and  [CORS-safelisted request-headers](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy"). Upon success, fetch will return an  [opaque filtered response](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy").      
"`navigate`"       This is a special mode used only when [navigating](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") between documents.      
"`websocket`"       This is a special mode used only when  [establishing a WebSocket connection](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy").      
"`webtransport`"       This is a special mode used only by `[WebTransport(url, options)](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer
Policy")`.         
Even though the default [request](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy") [mode](#local-scheme "Referrer Policy") is
"`no-cors`", standards are highly discouraged from using it for new features. It
is rather unsafe.

A [request](#http-scheme "Referrer Policy") has an associated*use-CORS-preflight
flag*. Unless statedotherwise, it is unset.

The [use-CORS-preflight flag](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") being set is one of several conditions that resultsin a [CORS-preflight request](#http-scheme "HTML Standard"). The [use-CORS-preflight flag](#fetch-scheme "HTML Standard") is set if either one or moreevent listeners are registered on an `[XMLHttpRequestUpload](#biblio-html "HTML Standard")`
object or if a `[ReadableStream](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by
default.")`object is used in a request.

A [request](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") has an
associated*credentials mode*,which is "`omit`", "`same-origin`", or"`include`". Unless stated otherwise,
it is "`same-origin`".


"`omit`"       Excludes credentials from this request, and causes any credentials
sent back in the response  to be ignored.       "`same-origin`"       Include credentials with
requests made to same-origin URLs, and use any credentials sent back  in responses
from same-origin URLs.       "`include`"       Always includes credentials with this request,
and always use any credentials sent back in the  response.         
[Request](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [credentials mode](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") controls the flow of [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") during a [fetch](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). When [request](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [mode](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
"`navigate`", its [credentials mode](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is assumed to be
"`include`" and [fetch](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") does not currently account for other values. If
*HTML* changes here, this standard will need corresponding changes.

A [request](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") has an
associated*use-URL-credentials flag*.Unless stated otherwise, it is unset.

When this flag is set, when a [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s[URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") has a [username](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and [password](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), and there is anavailable [authentication entry](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") for the [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then the [URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’scredentials are preferred over that of the [authentication entry](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). Modern specifications avoidsetting this flag, since putting credentials in [URLs](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is discouraged, but some olderfeatures set it for compatibility reasons.

A [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") has an
associated*cache mode*, which is"`default`", "`no-store`", "`reload`","`no-cache`", "`force-cache`", or"`only-if-cached`". Unless stated
otherwise, it is "`default`".


"`default`"       [Fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") will inspect the HTTP cache on the way to the network. If the HTTP cache  contains a matching [fresh response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") it will be returned. If the HTTP cache contains a  matching [stale-while-revalidate response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") it will be returned, and a conditional network  fetch will be made to update the entry in the HTTP cache. If the HTTP cache contains a matching  [stale response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), a conditional network fetch will be returned to update the entry in  the HTTP cache. Otherwise, a non-conditional network fetch will be returned to update the entry  in the HTTP cache. [[HTTP]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") [[STALE-WHILE-REVALIDATE]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")      
"`no-store`"       Fetch behaves as if there is no HTTP cache at all.       "`reload`"       Fetch
behaves as if there is no HTTP cache on the way to the network. Ergo, it creates
a  normal request and updates the HTTP cache with the response.       "`no-cache`"       
Fetch creates a conditional request if there is a response in the HTTP cache and
a normal  request otherwise. It then updates the HTTP cache with the response.   
"`force-cache`"       Fetch uses any response in the HTTP cache matching the request, not
paying attention to  staleness. If there was no response, it creates a normal request
and updates the HTTP cache with  the response.       "`only-if-cached`"       Fetch uses any response
in the HTTP cache matching the request, not paying attention to  staleness. If there
was no response, it returns a network error. (Can only be used when  [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) is
"`same-origin`". Any cached redirects will be followed assuming  [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css)’s  [redirect mode](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is
"`follow`" and the  redirects do not violate [request](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle")’s  [mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").)      

If [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [contains](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") \``If-Modified-Since`\`,
\``If-None-Match`\`, \``If-Unmodified-Since`\`, \``If-Match`\`, or \``If-Range`\`, [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") will set [cache mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to
"`no-store`" if it is "`default`".

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*redirect mode*, which
is"`follow`", "`error`", or "`manual`".Unless stated otherwise, it is "`follow`".


"`follow`"       Follow all redirects incurred when fetching a resource.      
"`error`"       Return a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") when a request is met with a redirect.      
"`manual`"       Retrieves an [opaque-redirect filtered response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") when a request is met with a redirect,  to allow a service worker to replay the redirect offline. The response is otherwise  indistinguishable from a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), to not violate  [atomic HTTP redirect handling](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").      

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated*integrity metadata*(a
string). Unless stated otherwise, it is the empty string.

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated*cryptographic nonce metadata*(a
string). Unless stated otherwise, it is the empty string.

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated*parser metadata*which
is the empty string, "`parser-inserted`", or"`not-parser-inserted`". Unless otherwise stated, it is the empty string.

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [cryptographic nonce metadata](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and[parser metadata](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") are generally populated from attributes and flags on the HTMLelement responsible for creating a [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). They are used by various algorithms
in*Content Security Policy* to determine whether requests or responses are to be
blocked ina given context. [[CSP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*reload-navigation flag*.Unless
stated otherwise, it is unset.

This flag is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*history-navigation
flag*.Unless stated otherwise, it is unset.

This flag is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated boolean *user-activation*.Unless
stated otherwise, it is false.

This is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated *WebDriver navigation
id*(null or a string). Unless stated otherwise, it is null.

This is for exclusive use by HTML’s navigate algorithm. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated boolean *render-blocking*.Unless
stated otherwise, it is false.

This flag is for exclusive use by HTML’s render-blocking mechanism. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated *WebTransport-hash
list* (a[WebTransport-hash list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise it is « ».

A *WebTransport-hash list* is a [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of zero or more[WebTransport-hashes](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

A *WebTransport-hash* is a [tuple](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")consisting of an *algorithm*
(a [string](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")) and a*value* (a [byte sequence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")).

This list is for exclusive use by `[WebTransport(url, options)](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` whenoptions
contains `[serverCertificateHashes](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.

---

A [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*URL list* (a [list](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle") of one ormore [URLs](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle")). Unless stated otherwise, it is a list containing a copy of[request](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key")’s [URL](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new/choose "You can also press the 'y' key").

A [request](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues "You can also press the 'y' key") has an
associated*current URL*. It is a pointer to thelast [URL](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") in [request](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese")’s [URL list](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese").

A [request](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese") has an associated*redirect
count*.Unless stated otherwise, it is zero.

A [request](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean") has an associated*response
tainting*,which is "`basic`", "`cors`", or "`opaque`".Unless stated otherwise, it is "`basic`".

A [request](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*prevent no-cache
cache-control header modification flag*.Unless stated otherwise, it is unset.

A [request](#url "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated *done flag*.Unless
stated otherwise, it is unset.

A [request](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*timing
allow failed flag*. Unless statedotherwise, it is unset.

A [request](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*navigation
timing allow values list* (a [list](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") of[lists](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [strings](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3")). Unless stated otherwise, it is « ».

Each [item](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3") in the [navigation timing allow values list](#network-partition-keys "Content Security Policy Level 3")holds the
\``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` values of one redirect [response](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") in anavigation’s redirect chain.

A [request](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [URL list](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [current URL](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3"),[redirect count](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [response tainting](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"),[done flag](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [timing allow failed flag](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), and[navigation timing allow values list](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3") are used as bookkeeping details by the[fetch](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") algorithm.

A [request](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an associated*WebDriver
id*which is the result of [generating a random UUID](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3"), set when the [request](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") iscreated. [[WEBCRYPTO]](#cors-protocol-and-credentials "Content Security Policy Level 3")

The [WebDriver id](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") is used by WebDriver-BiDi. It remains constantacross redirects, authentication attempts, and CORS-preflight fetches of an initial request.When a request is [cloned](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), the created request gets a unique[WebDriver id](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). [[WEBDRIVER-BIDI]](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")

---

A *subresource request* is a [request](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3")whose [destination](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`audio`", "`audioworklet`","`font`", "`image`", "`json`", "`manifest`","`paintworklet`", "`script`", "`style`", "`text`","`track`", "`video`", "`xslt`",
or the empty string.

A *non-subresource request* is a [request](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")whose [destination](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`document`", "`embed`","`frame`", "`iframe`", "`object`", "`report`","`serviceworker`", "`sharedworker`", or "`worker`".

A *navigation request* is a [request](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose[destination](#cors-preflight-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")
is"`document`", "`embed`", "`frame`", "`iframe`",or "`object`".

See [handle fetch](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") for usage of these terms.[[SW]](#tao-check "Content Security Policy Level 3")

---


To compute the *redirect-taint* of a[request](#fetch-api "Content Security Policy Level 3") request,
perform the following steps. They return"`same-origin`", "`same-site`", or "`cross-site`".


1.       [Assert](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3"): request’s [origin](#body-mixin "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`client`".

2.       Let lastURL be null.

3.       Let taint be "`same-origin`".

4.       [For each](#fetch-method "Content Security Policy Level 3") url of request’s [URL list](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3"):  


1.         If lastURL is null, then set lastURL to url and   [continue](#background-reading "Content Security Policy Level 3").   

2.         If url’s [origin](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not [same site](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3") with   lastURL’s [origin](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3") and request’s [origin](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   not [same site](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") with lastURL’s [origin](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return   "`cross-site`".   

3.         If url’s [origin](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not [same origin](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") with   lastURL’s [origin](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3") and request’s [origin](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   not [same origin](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") with lastURL’s [origin](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then set   taint to "`same-site`".   

4.         Set lastURL to url.  


-       Return taint.


*Serializing a request origin*, given a [request](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3")
request, is torun these steps:


1.       [Assert](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3"): request’s [origin](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`client`".

2.       If request’s [redirect-taint](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`same-origin`", then return "`null`".

3.       Return request’s [origin](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly"), [serialized](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content").


*Byte-serializing a request origin*, given a [request](#http-origin "The Web Origin Concept")
request,is to return the result of [serializing a request origin](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") with
request,[isomorphic encoded](#preface "Infra Standard").

---


To *clone* a[request](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") request, run these steps:


1.       Let newRequest be a copy of request, except for its [body](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") and [WebDriver id](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard").

2.       Set newRequest’s [WebDriver id](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") to the result of [generating a random UUID](#infrastructure "Infra Standard"). [[WEBCRYPTO]](#infrastructure "Infra Standard")

3.       If request’s [body](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard") is non-null, set newRequest’s [body](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") to the result of [cloning](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") request’s [body](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF").

4.       Return newRequest.


---


To *add a range header* to a[request](#biblio-url "URL Standard") request, with an
integer first, and an optional integerlast, run these steps:


1.       [Assert](#biblio-webtransport "WebTransport"): last is not given, or first is less than or equal to last.

2.       Let rangeValue be \``bytes=`\`.

3.       [Serialize](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") and [isomorphic encode](#authentication-entry "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") first, and append the result to rangeValue.

4.       Append 0x2D (-) to rangeValue.

5.       If last is given, then [serialize](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") and [isomorphic encode](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") it, and append the result to rangeValue.

6.       [Append](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics") (\``Range`\`, rangeValue) to request’s [header list](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing").


A range header denotes an inclusive byte range. There a range header wherefirst is
0 and last is 500, is a range of 501 bytes.

Features that combine multiple responses into one logical resource are historically
asource of security bugs. Please seek security review for features that deal with
partial responses.

---


To *serialize a response URL for reporting*, given a [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource
Timing")response, run these steps:


1.       [Assert](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"): response’s [URL list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") [is not empty](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").

2.       Let url be a copy of response’s [URL list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")[0].  

	This is not response’s [URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") in order to avoid  leaking information about redirect targets (see  [similar considerations for CSP reporting](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")  too). [[CSP]](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")

3.       [Set the username](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") given url and the empty string.

4.       [Set the password](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") given url and the empty string.

5.       Return the [serialization](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") of url with [*exclude fragment*](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource Timing") set to true.


To check if *Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy allows credentials*, given a[request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")
request, run these steps:


1.       [Assert](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"): request’s [origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is not "`client`".

2.       If request’s [mode](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is not "`no-cors`", then return true.

3.       If request’s [client](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is null, then return true.

4.       If request’s [client](#concept-response "Resource Timing")’s [policy container](#concept-response "Resource Timing")’s [embedder policy](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [value](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") is not "[`credentialless`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")", then return true.

5.       If request’s [origin](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is [same origin](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") with request’s [current URL](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [origin](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") and request’s [redirect-taint](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is not "`same-origin`", then return true.

6.       Return false.


#### 2.2.6. Responses

The result of [fetch](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is
a*response*. A [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")evolves over time. That is, not all its fields are available straight away.

A [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") has an
associated*type* which is"`basic`","`cors`","`default`","`error`","`opaque`", or"`opaqueredirect`".Unless stated otherwise,
it is "`default`".

A [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") can have an associated*aborted
flag*, which is initially unset.

This indicates that the request was intentionally aborted by the developer orend-user.

A [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*URL*. It is a
pointer to the last[URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") in [response](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing")’s [URL list](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") and null if[response](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing")’s [URL list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") [is empty](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").

A [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*URL list* (a
[list](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") of zero ormore [URLs](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")). Unless stated otherwise, it is « ».

Except for the first and last [URL](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing"), if any, a [response](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing")’s[URL list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is not directly exposed to script as that would violate[atomic HTTP redirect handling](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").

A [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*status*, which
is a [status](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing").Unless stated otherwise it is 200.

A [response](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing") has an associated*status
message*. Unless statedotherwise it is the empty byte sequence.

Responses over an HTTP/2 connection will always have the empty byte sequence as statusmessage
as HTTP/2 does not support them.

A [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*header list*
(a[header list](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")). Unless stated otherwise it is « ».

A [response](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing") has an associated*body*
(null or a[body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")). Unless stated otherwise it is null.

The [source](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") and [length](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") concepts of a network’s[response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")’s [body](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing") are always null.

A [response](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing") has an associated*cache
state* (the empty string,"`local`", or "`validated`"). Unless stated otherwise, it is the emptystring.

This is intended for usage by *Service Workers* and*Resource Timing*.  [[SW]](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing")

A [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing") has an associated*CORS-exposed
header-name list*(a list of zero or more [header](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing")[names](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing")). The list is empty unless otherwise specified.

A [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") will typically get its[CORS-exposed header-name list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") set by [extracting header values](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") from the\`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")\` header. This list is used by a[CORS filtered response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to determine which headers to expose.

A [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*range-requested
flag*, which isinitially unset.

This is used to prevent a partial response from an earlier ranged request beingprovided
to an API that didn’t make a range request. See the flag’s usage for a detailed
descriptionof the attack.

A [response](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated
*request-includes-credentials*(a boolean), which is initially true.

A [response](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") has an associated*timing
allow passed flag*,which is initially unset.

This is used so that the caller to a fetch can determine if sensitive timing data
isallowed on the resource fetched by looking at the flag of the response returned.
Because the flag onthe response of a redirect has to be set if it was set for previous
responses in the redirect chain,this is also tracked internally using the request’s
[timing allow failed flag](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing").

A [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") has an
associated*navigation timing allow values list* (a [list](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") of[lists](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") of [strings](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")). Unless stated otherwise, it is « ».

This holds the \``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` values of each redirect[response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") in a navigation’s redirect chain. It is used so that callers can determine,once the navigation’s destination origin is known, whether that origin is allowed by every redirectin the redirect chain.

A [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") has an
associated*body info*(a [response body info](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")). Unless stated otherwise, it is a new[response body info](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

A [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") has an
associated*service worker timing info* (null or a[service worker timing info](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")), which is initially null.

A [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") has an associated
*redirect taint*("`same-origin`", "`same-site`", or "`cross-site`"), which isinitially "`same-origin`".

---

A *network error* is a [response](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") whose[type](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") is
"`error`", [status](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") is 0,[status message](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is the empty byte sequence,[header list](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is « », [body](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is null, and[body info](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") is a new [response body info](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing").

An *aborted network error* is a[network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") whose [aborted flag](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") is set.


To create the *appropriate network error* given [fetch params](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource
Timing")fetchParams:


1.       [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing"): fetchParams is [canceled](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing").

2.       Return an [aborted network error](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing") if fetchParams is [aborted](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"); otherwise return a [network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy").


---

A *filtered response* is a [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")that offers a limited view on an associated [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"). This associated[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") can be accessed through [filtered response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer
Policy")’s*internal response* (a[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") that is neither a [network error](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") nor a[filtered response](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")).

Unless stated otherwise a [filtered response](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")’s associated concepts (such as its[body](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")) refer to the associated concepts of its[internal response](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy"). (The exceptions to this are listed below as partof defining the concrete types of [filtered responses](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy").)


The [fetch](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") algorithm by way of [*processResponse*](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy") and equivalent parameters exposes [filtered responses](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy") to callers to ensure they do not accidentally leak information. If the information needs to be revealed for legacy reasons, e.g., to feed image data to a decoder, the associated [internal response](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy") can be used by specification algorithms.

New specifications ought not to build further on [opaque filtered responses](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") or [opaque-redirect filtered responses](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy"). Those are legacy constructs and cannot always be adequately protected given contemporary computer architecture.

A *basic filtered response* is a[filtered response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy") whose[type](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy") is
"`basic`" and[header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") excludes any[headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") in[internal response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#networking-task-source "Referrer Policy")’s[header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") whose[name](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") is a[forbidden response-header name](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy").

A *CORS filtered response* is a[filtered response](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") whose[type](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") is
"`cors`" and[header list](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") excludes any[headers](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") in[internal response](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy")’s[header list](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") whose[name](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") is
*not* a[CORS-safelisted response-header name](#url "Referrer Policy"), given[internal response](#url "Referrer Policy")’s[CORS-exposed header-name list](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy").

An *opaque filtered response* is a[filtered response](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy") whose[type](#local-scheme "Referrer Policy") is
"`opaque`",[URL list](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy") is « »,[status](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy") is 0,[status message](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") is the empty byte sequence,[header list](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") is « »,[body](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") is null, and[body info](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") is a new [response body info](#http-scheme "HTML Standard").

An*opaque-redirect filtered response*is a [filtered response](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose[type](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
"`opaqueredirect`",[status](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is 0,[status message](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is the empty byte sequence,[header list](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is « »,[body](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is null, and[body info](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is a new [response body info](#http-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").


Exposing the [URL list](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") for [opaque-redirect filtered responses](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is harmless since no redirects are followed.

In other words, an [opaque filtered response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and an [opaque-redirect filtered response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") are nearly indistinguishable from a [network error](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). When introducing new APIs, do not use the [internal response](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") for internal specification algorithms as that will leak information.

This also means that JavaScript APIs, such as [`response.ok`](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), will return rather useless results.


The [type](#http-tab-or-space-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") of a [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is exposed to script through the `[type](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")`
getter:

```example
console.log(new Response().type); // "default"

console.log((await fetch("/")).type); // "basic"

console.log((await fetch("https://api.example/status")).type); // "cors"

console.log((await fetch("https://crossorigin.example/image", { mode: "no-cors" })).type); // "opaque"

console.log((await fetch("/surprise-me", { redirect: "manual" })).type); // "opaqueredirect"
```

(This assumes that the various resources exist, `https://api.example/status` has the appropriate CORS headers,
and `/surprise-me` uses a [redirect status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").)

---


To *clone* a[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")
response, run these steps:


1.       If response is a [filtered response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then return a new identical [filtered response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose [internal response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is a [clone](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") of response’s [internal response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

2.       Let newResponse be a copy of response, except for its [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

3.       If response’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is non-null, then set newResponse’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to the result of [cloning](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") response’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

4.       Return newResponse.


---

A *fresh response* is a [response](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose[current age](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is within its [freshness lifetime](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

A *stale-while-revalidate response* is a[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") that is not a [fresh response](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) and whose [current age](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) is within the[stale-while-revalidate lifetime](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle"). [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") [[STALE-WHILE-REVALIDATE]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

A *stale response* is a [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that isnot a [fresh response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") or a [stale-while-revalidate response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

---


The *location URL* of a[response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") response, given
null or an [ASCII string](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")requestFragment, is the
value returned by the following steps. They return null, failure,or a [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").


1.       If response’s [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [redirect status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then return null.

2.       Let location be the result of [extracting header list values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given \``Location`\` and response’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

3.       If location is a [header value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set location to the  result of [parsing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") location with response’s  [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").  

	If response was constructed through the `[Response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` constructor,  response’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") will be null, meaning that location will  only parse successfully if it is an [absolute-URL-with-fragment string](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

4.       If location is a [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose [fragment](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is null, then set  location’s [fragment](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to requestFragment.  

	This ensures that synthetic (indeed, all) responses follow the processing model for  redirects defined by HTTP. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

5.       Return location.


The [location URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") algorithm is exclusively used for redirecthandling in this standard and in
*HTML*’s navigate algorithm which handles redirectsmanually. [[HTML]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

#### 2.2.7. Miscellaneous

A *potential destination* is"`fetch`" or a [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") which is not the empty string.


To *translate* a[potential destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") potentialDestination,
run these steps:


1.       If potentialDestination is "`fetch`", then return the empty string.

2.       [Assert](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"): potentialDestination is a [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

3.       Return potentialDestination.


### 2.3. Authentication entries

An *authentication entry* and a *proxy-authentication entry* aretuples of username,
password, and realm, used for HTTP authentication and HTTP proxy authentication,and
associated with one or more [requests](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

User agents should allow both to be cleared together with HTTP cookies and similar
trackingfunctionality.

Further details are defined by HTTP. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")

### 2.4. Fetch groups

Each [environment settings object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has an associated*fetch
group*, which holds a [fetch group](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

A *fetch group* holds information about fetches.

A [fetch group](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") has associated:
*fetch records*     A [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of [fetch records](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    
*deferred fetch records*     A [list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of [deferred fetch records](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    

A *fetch record* is a [struct](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with the following[items](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"):
*request*     A [request](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle").     *controller*
A [fetch controller](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") or null.    

---

A *deferred fetch record* is a [struct](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key") used to maintain state needed toinvoke a fetch at a later time, e.g., when a document is unloaded or becomes not[fully active](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key"). It has the following [items](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese"):
*request*     A [request](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese").     *notify
invoked*     An algorithm accepting no arguments.     *invoke state* (default "`pending`")
"`pending`", "`sent`", or "`aborted`".      
---

When a [fetch group](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") fetchGroup is*terminated*:


1.      [For each](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") [fetch record](#methods "Content Security Policy Level 3") record of fetchGroup’s [fetch records](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3"), if record’s [controller](#statuses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is non-null and record’s [request](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [done flag](#responses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is unset and [keepalive](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3") is false, [terminate](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3") record’s [controller](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3").

2.      [Process deferred fetches](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3") for fetchGroup.


### 2.5. Resolving domains


[![(This is a tracking vector.)](idl "Content Security Policy Level 3")](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3")
To*resolve an origin*, given a[network partition key](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3")
key and an [origin](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") origin:


1.       If origin’s [host](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [IP address](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return « origin’s [host](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") ».

2.       If origin’s [host](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [public suffix](#http-requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`localhost`" or "`localhost.`", then return « `::1`, `127.0.0.1` ».

3.       Perform an [implementation-defined](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") operation to turn origin into a  [set](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") of one or more [IP addresses](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3").  

	It is also [implementation-defined](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") whether other operations might be performed to get  connection information beyond just [IP addresses](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). For example, if origin’s  [scheme](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [HTTP(S) scheme](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3"), the implementation might perform a DNS query  for HTTPS RRs. [[SVCB]](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")  

	If this operation succeeds, return the [set](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [IP addresses](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") and any  additional [implementation-defined](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3") information.

4.       Return failure.


The results of [resolve an origin](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") may be cached. If they are cached,
key shouldbe used as part of the cache key.


Typically this operation would involve DNS and as such caching can happen on DNS
servers without key being taken into account. Depending on the implementation it
might also not be possible to take key into account locally. [[RFC1035]](#http-network-or-cache-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")

The order of the [IP addresses](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") that the [resolve an origin](#cors-preflight-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") algorithm can return can differ between invocations.

The particulars (apart from the cache key) are not tied down as they are not pertinent
to the system the Fetch Standard establishes. Other documents ought not to build
on this primitive without having a considered discussion with the Fetch Standard
community first.

### 2.6. Connections

A user agent has an associated *connection pool*. A[connection pool](#headers-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") is an [ordered set](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3") of zero or
more*connections*. Each [connection](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") isidentified by an associated
*key* (a [network partition key](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3")),*origin*
(an [origin](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")), and *credentials*(a
boolean).

Each [connection](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3") has an
associated*timing info* (a[connection timing info](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3")).

A *connection timing info* is a [struct](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") used to maintain timinginformation pertaining to the process of obtaining a connection. It has the following[items](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3"):
*domain lookup start time* (default 0)     *domain lookup end time* (default
0)     *connection start time* (default 0)     *connection end time* (default 0)
*secure connection start time* (default 0)     A `[DOMHighResTimeStamp](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level
3")`.     *ALPN negotiated protocol* (default the empty [byte sequence](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly"))    
A [byte sequence](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content").      


To *clamp and coarsen connection timing info*, given a[connection timing info](#http-origin "The Web Origin Concept")
timingInfo, a `[DOMHighResTimeStamp](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept")`defaultStartTime, and
a boolean crossOriginIsolatedCapability, run thesesteps:


1.       If timingInfo’s [connection start time](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is less than defaultStartTime, then return a new [connection timing info](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") whose [domain lookup start time](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is defaultStartTime, [domain lookup end time](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is defaultStartTime, [connection start time](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is defaultStartTime, [connection end time](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") is defaultStartTime, [secure connection start time](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard") is defaultStartTime, and [ALPN negotiated protocol](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") is timingInfo’s [ALPN negotiated protocol](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF").

2.       Return a new [connection timing info](#biblio-html "HTML Standard") whose [domain lookup start time](#biblio-mimesniff "HTTP Semantics") is the result of [coarsen time](#biblio-url "Streams Standard") given timingInfo’s [domain lookup start time](#biblio-websockets "Web IDL Standard") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [domain lookup end time](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") is the result of [coarsen time](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") given timingInfo’s [domain lookup end time](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [connection start time](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") is the result of [coarsen time](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") given timingInfo’s [connection start time](#biblio-http "The Transport Layer Security (TLS) Protocol Version 1.3") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [connection end time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") is the result of [coarsen time](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") given timingInfo’s [connection end time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, [secure connection start time](#concept-request "Resource Timing") is the result of [coarsen time](#concept-request "Resource Timing") given timingInfo’s [connection end time](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") and crossOriginIsolatedCapability, and [ALPN negotiated protocol](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is timingInfo’s [ALPN negotiated protocol](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").


---

A *new connection setting* is "`no`", "`yes`", or"`yes-and-dedicated`".


To *obtain a connection*, given a[network partition key](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
key, [URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
url, booleancredentials, an optional [new connection setting](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
new (default"`no`"), an optional boolean*requireUnreliable* (default false), and anoptional
[WebTransport-hash list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource
Timing")*webTransportHashes* (default « »):


1.       If new is "`no`":  


1.         [Assert](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"): webTransportHashes [is empty](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").   

2.         Let connections be a set of [connections](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") in the user agent’s   [connection pool](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") whose [key](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is key,   [origin](#concept-response "Resource Timing") is url’s [origin](#concept-response "Resource Timing"), and   [credentials](#concept-response "Resource Timing") is credentials.   

3.         If connections is not empty and requireUnreliable is false, then   return one of connections.   

4.         If there is a [connection](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") capable of supporting unreliable transport in   connections, e.g., HTTP/3, then return that [connection](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing").  


-       Let proxies be the result of finding proxies for url in an  [implementation-defined](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") manner. If there are no proxies, let proxies be  « "`DIRECT`" ».  

	This is where non-standard technology such as  [Web Proxy Auto-Discovery Protocol (WPAD)](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")  and [proxy auto-config (PAC)](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") come  into play. The "`DIRECT`" value means to not use a proxy for this particular  url.

-       Let timingInfo be a new [connection timing info](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").

-       [For each](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") proxy of proxies:  


1.         Set timingInfo’s [domain lookup start time](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")   to the [unsafe shared current time](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing").   

2.         Let hosts be « url’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")’s   [host](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") ».   

3.         If proxy is "`DIRECT`", then set hosts to the result of   running [resolve an origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") given key and url’s [origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").   

4.         If hosts is failure, then [continue](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").   

5.         Set timingInfo’s [domain lookup end time](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to   the [unsafe shared current time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing").   

6.         Let connection be the result of running this step: run [create a connection](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing")    given key, url’s [origin](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing"), credentials,    proxy, an [implementation-defined](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") [host](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") from hosts,    timingInfo, requireUnreliable, and webTransportHashes an    [implementation-defined](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") number of times, [in parallel](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") from each other, and wait for    at least 1 to return a value. In an [implementation-defined](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") manner, select a value to    return from the returned values and return it. Any other returned values that are    [connections](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") may be closed.    

	Essentially this allows an implementation to pick one or more    [IP addresses](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") from the return value of [resolve an origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") (assuming    proxy is "`DIRECT`") and race them against each other, favor    [IPv6 addresses](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing"), retry in case of a timeout, etc.   

7.         If connection is failure, then [continue](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing").   

8.         If new is not "`yes-and-dedicated`", then [append](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing")   connection to the user agent’s [connection pool](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").   

9.         Return connection.  


-       Return failure.


This is intentionally a little vague as there are a lot of nuances to connectionmanagement
that are best left to the discretion of implementers. Describing this helps explain
the`<link rel=preconnect>` feature and clearly stipulates that [connections](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing") arekeyed on [credentials](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing"). The latter clarifies that, e.g., TLS session identifiers are notreused across [connections](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing") whose [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") are false with[connections](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") whose [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") are true.

---


To *create a connection*, given a [network partition key](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing")
key,[origin](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing") origin,
boolean credentials, string proxy,[host](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")
host, [connection timing info](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")
timingInfo,boolean requireUnreliable, and a [WebTransport-hash list](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource
Timing")webTransportHashes:


1.       Set timingInfo’s [connection start time](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing") to the [unsafe shared current time](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing").

2.       Let connection be a new [connection](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") whose [key](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") is  key, [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") is origin,  [credentials](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is credentials, and [timing info](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")  is timingInfo. [Record connection timing info](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") given connection  and use connection to establish an HTTP connection to host, taking  proxy and origin into account, with the following caveats: [[HTTP]](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")  [[HTTP1]](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") [[TLS]](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")  
3.         If requireUnreliable is true, then establish a connection capable of unreliable   transport, e.g., an HTTP/3 connection. [[HTTP3]](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing")   

4.         When establishing a connection capable of unreliable transport, enable options that are   necessary for WebTransport. For HTTP/3, this means including   `SETTINGS_ENABLE_WEBTRANSPORT` with a value of `1` and   `H3_DATAGRAM` with a value of `1` in the initial `SETTINGS`   frame. [[WEBTRANSPORT-HTTP3]](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") [[HTTP3-DATAGRAM]](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing")   

5.         If credentials is false, then do not send a TLS client certificate.   

6.         If webTransportHashes [is not empty](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing"), instead of using the default   certificate verification algorithm, consider the server certificate valid if it meets the   [custom certificate requirements](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") and if   [verifying the certificate hash](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") against webTransportHashes returns   true. If either condition is not met, then return failure.   

7.         If establishing a connection does not succeed (e.g., a UDP, TCP, or TLS error), then   return failure.  


8.       
Set timingInfo’s [ALPN negotiated protocol](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") to
connection’s ALPN Protocol ID, with the following caveats: [[RFC7301]](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")  

9.         When a proxy is configured, if a tunnel connection is established then this must be the   ALPN Protocol ID of the tunneled protocol, otherwise it must be the ALPN Protocol ID of the first   hop to the proxy.   

10.         In case the user agent is using an experimental, non-registered protocol, the user agent must    use the used ALPN Protocol ID, if any. If ALPN was not used for protocol negotiations, the user    agent may use another descriptive string.    

	timingInfo’s    [ALPN negotiated protocol](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is intended to identify the network    protocol in use regardless of how it was actually negotiated; that is, even if ALPN is not used    to negotiate the network protocol, this is the ALPN Protocol IDs that indicates the protocol in    use.  


IANA maintains a  [list of ALPN Protocol IDs](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

11.       
Return connection.


---


To *record connection timing info* given a [connection](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource
Timing")connection, let timingInfo be connection’s[timing info](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") and observe these requirements:

-       timingInfo’s [connection end time](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") should be the  [unsafe shared current time](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") immediately after establishing the connection to the  server or proxy, as follows:  
-         The returned time must include the time interval to establish the transport connection, as   well as other time intervals such as SOCKS authentication. It must include the time interval to   complete enough of the TLS handshake to request the resource.   

-         If the user agent used TLS False Start for this connection, this interval must not include   the time needed to receive the server’s Finished message. [[RFC7918]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing")   

-         If the user agent sends the request with early data without waiting for the full handshake   to complete, this interval must not include the time needed to receive the server’s ServerHello   message. [[RFC8470]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing")   

-         If the user agent waits for full handshake completion to send the request, this interval   includes the full TLS handshake even if other requests were sent using early data on   connection.  


Suppose the user agent establishes an HTTP/2  connection over TLS 1.3 to send a `GET`
request and a `POST` request. It  sends the ClientHello at time t1 and then sends the
`GET` request with early  data. The `POST` request is not safe ([[HTTP]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing"), section 9.2.1), so the user  agent waits to complete the handshake at time
t2 before sending it. Although both  requests used the same connection, the `GET` request
reports a connection end time of  t1, while the `POST` request reports t2.

-       
If a secure transport is used, timingInfo’s [secure connection start time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") should be the result of calling [unsafe shared current time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") immediately before starting the handshake process to secure
connection. [[TLS]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")

-       
If connection is an HTTP/3 connection, timingInfo’s [connection start time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") and
timingInfo’s [secure connection start time](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") must be equal. (In HTTP/3 the secure transport handshake process is performed as part of the initial connection setup.) [[HTTP3]](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")


The [clamp and coarsen connection timing info](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") algorithm ensures thatdetails of reused connections are not exposed and time values are coarsened.

### 2.7. Network partition keys

A *network partition key* is a tuple consisting of a [site](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") and nullor an [implementation-defined](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy") value.


To *determine the network partition key*, given an[environment](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy")
environment:


1.       Let topLevelOrigin be environment’s [top-level origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy").

2.       If topLevelOrigin is null, then set topLevelOrigin to environment’s [top-level creation URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy")’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy").

3.       [Assert](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy"): topLevelOrigin is an [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy").

4.       Let topLevelSite be the result of [obtaining a site](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy"), given topLevelOrigin.

5.       Let secondKey be null or an [implementation-defined](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") value.  

	The second key is intentionally a little vague as the finer points are still  evolving. See [issue #1035](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy").

6.       Return (topLevelSite, secondKey).


To *determine the network partition key*, given a [request](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer
Policy")request:


1.
