If request’s [reserved client](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") is non-null, then return the result of [determining the network partition key](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") given request’s [reserved client](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy").

2.       If request’s [client](#url "Referrer Policy") is non-null, then return the result of [determining the network partition key](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy") given request’s [client](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy").

3.       Return null.


### 2.8. HTTP cache partitions


To *determine the HTTP cache partition*, given a [request](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") request:


1.       Let key be the result of [determining the network partition key](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") given request.

2.       If key is null, then return null.

3.       Return the unique HTTP cache associated with key. [[HTTP-CACHING]](#http-scheme "HTML Standard")


### 2.9. Port blocking

New protocols can avoid the need for blocking ports by negotiating the protocolthrough
TLS using ALPN. The protocol cannot be spoofed through HTTP requests in that case.[[RFC7301]](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")


To determine whether fetching a [request](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")
request*should be blocked due to a bad port*:


1.       Let url be request’s [current URL](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

2.       If url’s [scheme](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is an [HTTP(S) scheme](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and url’s [port](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is a [bad port](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then return **blocked**.

3.       Return **allowed**.


A [port](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
a*bad port* if it is listed in the first column of the following table.

### 2.10. Shouldresponse to request be blocked due to its MIME type?

Run these steps:


1.       Let mimeType be the result of [extracting a MIME type](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") from response’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

2.       If mimeType is failure, then return **allowed**.

3.       Let destination be request’s [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

4.       If destination is [script-like](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and one of the  following is true, then return **blocked**:  
5. mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [starts with](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   "`audio/`", "`image/`", or "`video/`".          
6. mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`text/csv`".        

7.       
Return **allowed**.


## 3. HTTP extensions

### 3.1. Cookies

The \``Cookie`\` request header and \``Set-Cookie`\` response headers arelargely defined in their
own specifications. We define additional infrastructure to be able to usethem conveniently
here. [[COOKIES]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

#### 3.1.1. \``Cookie`\` header


To *append a request \``Cookie`\` header*, given a [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")request:


1.       If the user agent is configured to disable cookies for request, then it should return.

2.       Let sameSite be the result of [determining the same-site mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for request.

3.       Let isSecure be true if request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`https`"; otherwise false.

4.       Let httpOnlyAllowed be true.  

	True follows from this being invoked from [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), as opposed to the  `document.cookie` getter steps for instance.

5.       Let cookies be the result of running [retrieve cookies](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given isSecure,  request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), request’s  [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [path](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), httpOnlyAllowed, and sameSite.  

	The cookie store returns an ordered list of cookies

6.       If cookies [is empty](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then return.

7.       Let value be the result of running [serialize cookies](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given cookies.

8.       [Append](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") (\``Cookie`\`, value) to request’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").


#### 3.1.2. \``Set-Cookie`\` header


To *parse and store response \``Set-Cookie`\` headers*, given a[request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")
request and a [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") response:


1.       If the user agent is configured to disable cookies for request, then it should return.

2.       Let allowNonHostOnlyCookieForPublicSuffix be false.

3.       Let isSecure be true if request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`https`"; otherwise false.

4.       Let httpOnlyAllowed be true.  

	True follows from this being invoked from [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), as opposed to the  `document.cookie` getter steps for instance.

5.       Let sameSiteStrictOrLaxAllowed be true if the result of [determine the same-site mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for request is "`strict-or-less`"; otherwise false.

6.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") header of response’s  [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"):  


1.         If header’s [name](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [byte-case-insensitive](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") match   for \``Set-Cookie`\`, then [continue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

2.         [Parse and store a cookie](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given header’s [value](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),   isSecure, request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [host](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),   request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [path](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), httpOnlyAllowed,   allowNonHostOnlyCookieForPublicSuffix, and sameSiteStrictOrLaxAllowed.   

3.         [Garbage collect cookies](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given request’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s   [host](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle").  


As noted elsewhere the \``Set-Cookie`\` header cannot be combined and  therefore each occurrence is processed independently. This is not allowed for any other header.


#### 3.1.3. Cookie infrastructure


To *determine the same-site mode* for a given [request](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "Japanese")
request:


1.       [Assert](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese"): request’s [method](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese") is "`GET`" or "`POST`".

2.       If request’s [top-level navigation initiator origin](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese") is not null and is not [same site](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Content Security Policy Level 3") with request’s [URL](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [origin](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return "`unset-or-less`".

3.       If request’s [method](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`GET`" and request’s [destination](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "document", then return "`lax-or-less`".

4.       If request’s [client](#url "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [has cross-site ancestor](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") is true, then return "`unset-or-less`".

5.       If request’s [redirect-taint](#statuses "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`cross-site`", then return "`unset-or-less`".

6.       Return "`strict-or-less`".


To obtain a *serialized cookie default path* given a [URL](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level
3")url:


1.       Let cloneURL be a clone of url.

2.       Set cloneURL’s [path](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3") to the [cookie default path](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3") of cloneURL’s [path](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3").

3.       Return the [URL path serialization](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") of cloneURL.


### 3.2. \``Origin`\` header

The \`*`Origin`*\`request [header](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") indicates where a[fetch](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") originates from.

The \`[`Origin`](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3")\` header is a version of
the\``Referer`\` [sic] header that does not reveal a [path](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). It is used for all[HTTP fetches](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") whose [request](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s[response tainting](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3") is
"`cors`", as well as those where[request](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [method](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") is neither
\``GET`\` nor\``HEAD`\`. Due to compatibility constraints it is not included in all[fetches](#cors-protocol-and-credentials "Content Security Policy Level 3").

Its possible [values](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") are all the return values of[byte-serializing a request origin](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3"), given a [request](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"). These are represented bythe following [ABNF](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"):

```secno
*serialized-ipv4*   = [dec-octet](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." [dec-octet](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." [dec-octet](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." [dec-octet](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")
*dec-octet*         = DIGIT                 ; 0-9
                  / %x31-39 DIGIT         ; 10-99
                  / "1" 2DIGIT            ; 100-199
                  / "2" %x30-34 DIGIT     ; 200-249
                  / "25" %x30-35          ; 250-255

*serialized-ipv6*   =                            7( h16 ":" ) h16
                  /                       "::" 5( h16 ":" ) h16
                  / [               h16 ] "::" 4( h16 ":" ) h16
                  / [ *1( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::" 3( h16 ":" ) h16
                  / [ *2( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::" 2( h16 ":" ) h16
                  / [ *3( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::"    h16 ":"   h16
                  / [ *4( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::"              h16
                  / [ *5( h16 ":" ) h16 ] "::"
*h16*               = "0" / ( non-zero-hex 0*3hex )
*non-zero-hex*      = %x31-39 / %x61-66 ; '1'-'9' or lowercase 'a'-'f'
*hex*               = %x30-39 / %x61-66 ; '0'-'9' or lowercase 'a'-'f

*lower-alpha*       = %x61-7A
*lower-alphanum*    = [lower-alpha](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level 3") / DIGIT
*domain-label*      = [lower-alphanum](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") / ( [lower-alphanum](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3") *( [lower-alphanum](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3") / "-" ) [lower-alphanum](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") )
*serialized-domain* = *( [domain-label](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") "." ) [domain-label](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3")

*serialized-scheme* = [lower-alpha](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3") *( [lower-alphanum](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") / "+" / "-" / "." )
*serialized-host*   = [serialized-ipv4](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers") / "[" [serialized-ipv6](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly") "]" / [serialized-domain](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content")
*serialized-port*   = 1*5DIGIT

*serialized-origin* = [serialized-scheme](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") "://" [serialized-host](#preface "The Web Origin Concept") [ ":" [serialized-port](#preface "Infra Standard") ]
*origin-or-null*    = [serialized-origin](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") / %s"null" ; case-sensitive

Origin = [origin-or-null](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard")```
   

    
This supplants the definition in *The Web Origin Concept*. [[ORIGIN]](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard")
    
The origin serialization defined here is more constrained than [[RFC3986]](#infrastructure "Infra Standard")’s grammar in twosubstantial ways. First, scheme and domains serializations are all lower case ASCII, withoutpercent encoding. Second, following the recommendations of
[*URL* § 3.6 Host serializing](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") and [[RFC5952]](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard"),IPv6 addresses are limited as follows:
    
     - The least-significant digits cannot be represented as an IPv4 address.       
- Leading zeros are forbidden.       
- All hex characters are lowercase.       
- "::" can’t elide only a single "0" block, so we allow at most 6 blocks when "::" is present.    
      
---
   

    
To *append a request \``Origin`\` header*,given a [request](#biblio-streams "Streams Standard") request, 
run these steps:
   

     1.       [Assert](#biblio-webidl "Web IDL Standard"): request’s [origin](#biblio-webtransport "WebTransport") is not "`client`". 
     
2.       Let serializedOrigin be the result of [byte-serializing a request origin](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") with request. 
     
3.       If request’s [response tainting](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") is "`cors`" or request’s [mode](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") is either "`websocket`" or "`webtransport`", then [append](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") (\``Origin`\`, serializedOrigin) to request’s [header list](#biblio-http "The Transport Layer Security (TLS) Protocol Version 1.3"). 
     
4.       Otherwise, if request’s [method](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") is neither \``GET`\` nor  \``HEAD`\`, then:  
     

       1.         If request’s [mode](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") is not "`cors`",    then switch on request’s [referrer policy](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing"):    
                 "`no-referrer`"                        Set serializedOrigin to \``null`\`.     
         "`no-referrer-when-downgrade`"              "`strict-origin`"              "`strict-origin-when-cross-origin`"                        If request’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is a [tuple origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), its     scheme is "`https`", and request’s     [current URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s scheme is not "`https`", then set     serializedOrigin to \``null`\`.     
         "`same-origin`"                        If request’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is not [same origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") with     request’s [current URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), then set     serializedOrigin to \``null`\`.     
         Otherwise              Do nothing.                   
2.         [Append](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") (\``Origin`\`, serializedOrigin) to   request’s [header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").  
      

    

    
A [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource Timing")’s [referrer policy](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is taken into account forall fetches where the fetcher did not explicitly opt into sharing their [origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") with theserver, e.g., via using the [CORS protocol](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").
      
### 3.3. CORS protocol
   
To allow sharing responses cross-origin and allow for more versatile[fetches](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") than possible with HTML’s`[form](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")` 
element, the *CORS protocol* exists. Itis layered on top of HTTP and allows responses 
to declare they can be shared with other[origins](#concept-response "Resource Timing").
   
It needs to be an opt-in mechanism to prevent leaking data from responses behind 
afirewall (intranets). Additionally, for [requests](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") including[credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") it needs to be opt-in to prevent leaking potentially-sensitive data.
   
This section explains the [CORS protocol](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") as it pertains to server developers.Requirements for user agents are part of the [fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") algorithm,except for the [new HTTP header syntax](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").
   
#### 3.3.1. General
   
The [CORS protocol](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") consists of a set of headers that indicates whether a response canbe shared cross-origin.
   
For [requests](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") that are more involved than what is possible with HTML’s `[form](#fetch-timing-info "Resource
Timing")`element, a [CORS-preflight request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is performed, to ensure [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s[current URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") supports the [CORS protocol](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing").
   
#### 3.3.2. HTTP requests
   
A *CORS request* is an HTTP request that includes an\`[`Origin`](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")\` header. It cannot be reliably identified as participatingin the [CORS protocol](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") as the \`[`Origin`](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")\` header is also includedfor all [requests](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") whose [method](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is neither
\``GET`\` nor\``HEAD`\`.
   
A *CORS-preflight request* is a [CORS request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")that checks to see if the [CORS protocol](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") is understood. It uses
\``OPTIONS`\` as[method](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") and includes the following [header](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing"):
       \`*`Access-Control-Request-Method`*\`          
Indicates which [method](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") a future [CORS request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to the same resource might use.
      
A [CORS-preflight request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") can also include the following [header](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"):
       \`*`Access-Control-Request-Headers`*\`          
Indicates which [headers](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") a future [CORS request](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") to the same resource might use.
      
#### 3.3.3. HTTP responses
   
An HTTP response to a [CORS request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") can include the following[headers](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"):
       \`*`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`*\`          
Indicates whether the response can be shared, via returning the literal [value](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing") of the \`[`Origin`](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing")\` request [header](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") (which can be
\``null`\`) or \``*`\` in a response. 
    \`*`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`*\`          
Indicates whether the response can be shared when [request](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")’s  [credentials mode](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") is 
"`include`".  
     
For a [CORS-preflight request](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing"), [request](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing")’s  [credentials mode](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing") is always
"`same-origin`", i.e., it excludes  credentials, but for any subsequent [CORS requests](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") it might not be. Support therefore needs  to be indicated as part of the HTTP response to the [CORS-preflight request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") as well.
      
An HTTP response to a [CORS-preflight request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") can include the following[headers](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing"):
       \`*`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`*\`          
Indicates which [methods](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") are supported by the [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")’s  [URL](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") for the purposes of the [CORS protocol](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing").  
     
The \``Allow`\` [header](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing") is  not relevant for the purposes of the [CORS protocol](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing"). 
    \`*`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`*\`          
Indicates which [headers](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") are supported by the [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing")’s [URL](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") for the purposes of the [CORS protocol](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"). 
    \`*`Access-Control-Max-Age`*\`          
Indicates the number of seconds (5 by default) the information provided by the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")\` and \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")\` [headers](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") can be cached.
      
An HTTP response to a [CORS request](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") that is not a[CORS-preflight request](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") can also include the following[header](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing"):
       \`*`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`*\`          
Indicates which [headers](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") can be exposed as part of the response by listing their [names](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing").
      
---
   
A successful HTTP response, i.e., one where the server developer intends to share 
it, to a[CORS request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") can use any [status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), as long as it includes the [headers](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")stated above with [values](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") matching up with the request.
   
A successful HTTP response to a [CORS-preflight request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") is similar, except it is restrictedto an [ok status](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing"), e.g., 200 or 204.
   
Any other kind of HTTP response is not successful and will either end up not being 
shared or failthe [CORS-preflight request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"). Be aware that any work the server performs might nonetheless leakthrough side channels, such as timing. If server developers wish to denote this explicitly, the 403[status](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") can be used, coupled with omitting the relevant [headers](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").
   
If desired, “failure” could also be shared, but that would make it a successful 
HTTPresponse. That is why for a successful HTTP response to a [CORS request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") that is not a[CORS-preflight request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") the [status](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") can be anything, including 403.
   
Ultimately server developers have a lot of freedom in how they handle HTTP responses 
and thesetactics can differ between the response to the [CORS-preflight request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") and the[CORS request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") that follows it:
   
    -      They can provide a static response. This can be helpful when working with caching intermediaries. A static response can both be successful and not successful depending on the [CORS request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"). This is okay. 
    
-      They can provide a dynamic response, tuned to [CORS request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"). This can be helpful when the response body is to be tailored to a specific origin or a response needs to have credentials and be successful for a set of origins.
   
   
#### 3.3.4. HTTP new-header syntax
   
[ABNF](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") for the [values](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") of the[headers](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") used by the [CORS protocol](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing"):

```dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
Access-Control-Request-Method    = [method](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing")
Access-Control-Request-Headers   = 1#[field-name](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing")

*wildcard*                         = "*"
Access-Control-Allow-Origin      = [origin-or-null](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") / [wildcard](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing")

Access-Control-Allow-Credentials = %s"true" ; case-sensitive
Access-Control-Expose-Headers    = #[field-name](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")
Access-Control-Max-Age           = [delta-seconds](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")
Access-Control-Allow-Methods     = #[method](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")
Access-Control-Allow-Headers     = #[field-name](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing")```

For \``Access-Control-Expose-Headers`\`,\``Access-Control-Allow-Methods`\`, and \``Access-Control-Allow-Headers`\`response [headers](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"), the [value](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")
\``*`\` counts as a wildcard for[requests](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") without [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"). For such [requests](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") there is noway to solely match a [header name](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") or [method](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") that is
\``*`\`.

#### 3.3.5. CORS protocol and credentials

When [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s[credentials mode](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") is
"`include`" ithas an impact on the functioning of the [CORS protocol](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") other than including[credentials](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") in the [fetch](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy").


In the old days, `[XMLHttpRequest](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy")` could be used
to set [request](#fetch-timing-info-post-redirect-start-time "Referrer Policy")’s [credentials mode](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") to
"`include`":

```algorithm
var client = new XMLHttpRequest()
client.open("GET", "./")
client.withCredentials = true
/* … */
```

Nowadays, `fetch("./", { credentials:"include" }).then(/* … */)` suffices.

A [request](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy")’s[credentials mode](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") is not necessarily observableon the server; only when [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") exist for a[request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") can it be observed by virtue of the[credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy") being included. Note that even so, a [CORS-preflight request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy")never includes [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy").

The server developer therefore needs to decide whether or not responses "tainted"
with[credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy") can be shared. And also needs to decide if[requests](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") necessitating a [CORS-preflight request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") caninclude [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#networking-task-source "Referrer Policy"). Generally speaking, both sharing responses and allowing requestswith [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") is rather unsafe, and extreme care has to be taken to avoid the[confused deputy problem](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy").

To share responses with [credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy"), the\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy")\` and\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy")\` [headers](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") areimportant. The following table serves to illustrate the various legal and illegal combinations for arequest to
`https://rabbit.invalid/`:

|   |
||------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| - | -                            | -                                                                                                 | -                                                                                                      | -       |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``*`\`                                                                                           | Omitted                                                                                                | ✅     | —​                                                                                                                                                         |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``*`\`                                                                                           | \``true`\`                                                                                             | ✅     | If credentials mode is not "`include`", then  \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy")\` is ignored. |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``https://rabbit.invalid/`\`                                                                     | Omitted                                                                                                | ❌     | A [serialized](#local-scheme "Referrer Policy") origin has no trailing slash.                                                                                  |
|   | "`omit`"                     | \``https://rabbit.invalid`\`                                                                      | Omitted                                                                                                | ✅     | —​                                                                                                                                                         |
|   | "`include`"                  | \``*`\`                                                                                           | \``true`\`                                                                                             | ❌     | If credentials mode is "`include`", then  \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#http-scheme "HTML Standard")\` cannot be  \``*`\`.                                |
|   | "`include`"                  | \``https://rabbit.invalid`\`                                                                      | \``true`\`                                                                                             | ✅     | —​                                                                                                                                                         |
|   | "`include`"                  | \``https://rabbit.invalid`\`                                                                      | \``True`\`                                                                                             | ❌     | \``true`\` is (byte) case-sensitive.                                                                                                                           |

Similarly, \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\`,\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\`, and\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` response headers can only
use\``*`\` as value when [request](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [credentials mode](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
not"`include`".

#### 3.3.6. Examples


A script at `https://foo.invalid/` wants to fetch some data from `https://bar.invalid/`. (Neither [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") nor response header access is important.)

```dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
var url = "https://bar.invalid/api?key=730d67a37d7f3d802e96396d00280768773813fbe726d116944d814422fc1a45&data=about:unicorn";
fetch(url).then(success, failure)
```

This will use the [CORS protocol](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), though this is entirely transparent to the developer from
`foo.invalid`. As part of the [CORS protocol](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), the user agent will include the \`[`Origin`](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` header in the request:

```algorithm
Origin: https://foo.invalid
```

Upon receiving a response from `bar.invalid`, the user agent will verify the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#http-tab-or-space-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` response header. If its value is either
\``https://foo.invalid`\` or \``*`\`, the user agent will invoke the `success` callback.
If it has any other value, or is missing, the user agent will invoke the `failure` callback.


The developer of `foo.invalid` is back, and now wants to fetch some data from `bar.invalid` while also
accessing a response header.

```example
fetch(url).then(response => {
  var hsts = response.headers.get("strict-transport-security"),
      csp = response.headers.get("content-security-policy")
  log(hsts, csp)
})
```

`bar.invalid` provides a correct \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` response header per the earlier example. The values of `hsts`
and `csp` will depend on the \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` response header. For example, if the response included the following headers

```example
Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'
Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubdomains; preload
Access-Control-Expose-Headers: Content-Security-Policy
```

then `hsts` would be null and `csp` would be "`default-src 'self'`", even though the response did include
both headers. This is because `bar.invalid` needs to explicitly share each header by listing
their names in the \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` response header.

Alternatively, if `bar.invalid` wanted to share all its response headers, for requests that
do not include [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), it could use
\``*`\` as value for the \`[`Access-Control-Expose-Headers`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")\` response header. If the request would have included [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), the response header names would have to be listed explicitly and
\``*`\` could not be used.


The developer of `foo.invalid` returns, now fetching some data from `bar.invalid` while including [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). This time around the [CORS protocol](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is no longer transparent to the developer as [credentials](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") require an explicit opt-in:

```tablenote
fetch(url, { credentials:"include" }).then(success, failure)
```

This also makes any \``Set-Cookie`\` response headers `bar.invalid` includes fully functional (they are
ignored otherwise).

The user agent will make sure to include any relevant [credentials](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") in the request. It will also put stricter requirements on the response. Not only will `bar.invalid`
need to list \``https://foo.invalid`\` as value for the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css)\` header
(\``*`\` is not allowed when [credentials](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css "subtitle") are involved), the \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle")\` header has to be present too:

```h-entry status-LS
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://foo.invalid
Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true
```

If the response does not include those two headers with those values, the `failure` callback
will be invoked. However, any \``Set-Cookie`\` response headers will be respected.

#### 3.3.7. CORS protocol exceptions

Specifications have allowed limited exceptions to the CORS safelist for non-safelisted\``Content-Type`\`
header values. These exceptions are made for requests that can betriggered by web
content but whose headers and bodies can be only minimally controlled by the webcontent.
Therefore, servers should expect cross-origin web content to be allowed to triggernon-preflighted
requests with the following non-safelisted \``Content-Type`\` headervalues:

- \``application/csp-report`\` [[CSP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")     
- \``application/expect-ct-report+json`\` [[RFC9163]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")     
- \``application/xss-auditor-report`\`     
- \``application/ocsp-request`\` [[RFC6960]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   

Specifications should avoid introducing new exceptions and should only do so with
carefulconsideration for the security consequences. New exceptions can be proposed
by[filing an issue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

### 3.4. \``Content-Length`\` header

The \``Content-Length`\` header is largely defined in HTTP. Its processing model isdefined here
as the model defined in HTTP is not compatible with web content. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")


To *extract a length*from a [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") headers,
run these steps:


1.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") \``Content-Length`\` from headers.

2.       If values is null, then return null.

3.       Let candidateValue be null.

4.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") value of values:  


1.         If candidateValue is null, then set candidateValue to   value.   

2.         Otherwise, if value is not candidateValue, return failure.  


-       If candidateValue is the empty string or has a [code point](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that is not an [ASCII digit](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then return null.

-       Return candidateValue, interpreted as decimal number.


### 3.5. \``Content-Type`\` header

The \``Content-Type`\` header is largely defined in HTTP. Its processing model isdefined here
as the model defined in HTTP is not compatible with web content. [[HTTP]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")


To*extract a MIME type*from a [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") headers,
run these steps. They return failure or a[MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").


1.       Let charset be null.

2.       Let essence be null.

3.       Let mimeType be null.

4.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") \``Content-Type`\` from headers.

5.       If values is null, then return failure.

6.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") value of values:  


1.         Let temporaryMimeType be the result of [parsing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   value.   

2.         If temporaryMimeType is failure or its [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is   "`*/*`", then [continue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

3.         Set mimeType to temporaryMimeType.   

4.         If mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not essence, then:    


1.           Set charset to null.     

2.           If mimeType’s [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"]     [exists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set charset to mimeType’s     [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"].     

3.           Set essence to mimeType’s [essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    


-         Otherwise, if mimeType’s   [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"] does not [exist](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), and   charset is non-null, set mimeType’s   [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")["`charset`"] to charset.  


-       If mimeType is null, then return failure.

-       Return mimeType.


When [extract a MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") returns failure or a [MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose[essence](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is incorrect for a given format, treat this as a fatal error.Existing web platform features have not always followed this pattern, which has been a major sourceof security vulnerabilities in those features over the years. In contrast, a[MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [parameters](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") can typically be safely ignored.


This is how [extract a MIME type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") functions in practice:

|                                                        |
||-----------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| -                                                      | -                           |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/plain;charset=gbk, text/html        |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html`                 |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html;charset=gbk;a=b, text/html;x=y |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html;x=y;charset=gbk` |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html;charset=gbk;a=b                |
| Content-Type: text/html;x=y                            |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html;charset=gbk                    |
| Content-Type: x/x                                      |
| Content-Type: text/html;x=y                            |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html;x=y`             |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html                                |
| Content-Type: cannot-parse                             |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        | `text/html`                 |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html                                |
| Content-Type: */*                                      |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        |
|                                                        |                             |
| ```p-summary                                           |
| Content-Type: text/html                                |
| Content-Type:                                          |
| ```                                                    |
|                                                        |


To *legacy extract an encoding* given failure or a [MIME type](#goals "Content Security Policy Level
3")mimeType and an [encoding](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") fallbackEncoding,
run these steps:


1.       If mimeType is failure, then return fallbackEncoding.

2.       If mimeType["`charset`"] does not [exist](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return fallbackEncoding.

3.       Let tentativeEncoding be the result of [getting an encoding](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") from mimeType["`charset`"].

4.       If tentativeEncoding is failure, then return fallbackEncoding.

5.       Return tentativeEncoding.


This algorithm allows mimeType to be failure so it can be more easily combined with
[extract a MIME type](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3").

It is denoted as legacy as modern formats are to exclusively use [UTF-8](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3").

### 3.6. \``X-Content-Type-Options`\` header

The\`*`X-Content-Type-Options`*\`response [header](#network-partition-keys "Content Security Policy Level 3") can be used to require checking of a [response](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level
3")’s\``Content-Type`\` [header](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") against the [destination](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") of a[request](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3").


To *determine nosniff*, given a [header list](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3") list,
runthese steps:


1.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") \`[`X-Content-Type-Options`](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3")\` from list.

2.       If values is null, then return false.

3.       If values[0] is an [ASCII case-insensitive](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for "`nosniff`", then return true.

4.       Return false.


Web developers and conformance checkers must use the following [value](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3")[ABNF](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") for \`[`X-Content-Type-Options`](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3")\`:

```secno
X-Content-Type-Options           = "nosniff" ; case-insensitive
```


#### 3.6.1. Shouldresponse to request be blocked due to nosniff?

Run these steps:


1.       If [determine nosniff](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") with response’s [header list](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is false, then return **allowed**.

2.       Let mimeType be the result of [extracting a MIME type](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") from response’s [header list](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3").

3.       Let destination be request’s [destination](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3").

4.       If destination is [script-like](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") and mimeType is failure or is not a [JavaScript MIME type](#http-redirect-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return **blocked**.

5.       If destination is "`style`" and mimeType is failure or its [essence](#http-network-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`text/css`", then return **blocked**.

6.       Return **allowed**.


Only [request](#cors-preflight-cache "Content Security Policy Level 3") [destinations](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") that are[script-like](#tao-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") or
"`style`" are considered as any exploitspertain to them. Also, considering "`image`"
was not compatible with deployed content.

### 3.7. \``Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`\` header

The\`*`Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`*\`response [header](#body-mixin "Content Security Policy Level 3") can be used to require checking a [request](#request-class "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s[current URL](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [origin](#fetch-method "Content Security Policy Level 3") against a [request](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s[origin](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3") when [request](#background-reading "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [mode](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")
is"`no-cors`".

Its [value](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3") [ABNF](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3"):

```secno
Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy     = %s"same-origin" / %s"same-site" / %s"cross-origin" ; case-sensitive
```


To perform a *cross-origin resource policy check*, given an [origin](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level
3")origin, an [environment settings object](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")
settingsObject, a stringdestination, a [response](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3")
response, and an optional booleanforNavigation, run these steps:


1.       Set forNavigation to false if it is not given.

2.       Let embedderPolicy be settingsObject’s [policy container](#index-defined-here "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [embedder policy](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3").

3.       If the [cross-origin resource policy internal check](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") with origin,  "[`unsafe-none`](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3")", response, and  forNavigation returns **blocked**, then return **blocked**.  

	This step is needed because we don’t want to report violations not related to  Cross-Origin Embedder Policy below.

4.       If the [cross-origin resource policy internal check](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") with origin, embedderPolicy’s [report-only value](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers"), response, and forNavigation returns **blocked**, then [queue a cross-origin embedder policy CORP violation report](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content") with response, settingsObject, destination, and true.

5.       If the [cross-origin resource policy internal check](#http-origin "Referrer Policy") with origin, embedderPolicy’s [value](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept"), response, and forNavigation returns **allowed**, then return **allowed**.

6.       [Queue a cross-origin embedder policy CORP violation report](#preface "Infra Standard") with response, settingsObject, destination, and false.

7.       Return **blocked**.


Only HTML’s navigate algorithm uses this check with forNavigation set totrue, and
it’s always for nested navigations. Otherwise, response is either the[internal response](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") of an [opaque filtered response](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") or a[response](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") which will be the [internal response](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") of an[opaque filtered response](#infrastructure "Infra Standard"). [[HTML]](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard")


To perform a *cross-origin resource policy internal check*, given an[origin](#biblio-encoding "Encoding Standard")
origin, an [embedder policy value](#biblio-html "HTML Standard")embedderPolicyValue,
a [response](#biblio-streams "MIME Sniffing Standard") response, and a booleanforNavigation,
run these steps:


1.       If forNavigation is true and embedderPolicyValue is "[`unsafe-none`](#biblio-websockets "WebSockets Standard")", then return **allowed**.

2.       Let policy be the result of [getting](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF")  \`[`Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF")\` from response’s  [header list](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism").  

	This means that \``Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-site, same-origin`\`  ends up as **allowed** below as it will never match anything, as long as  embedderPolicyValue is "[`unsafe-none`](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism")".  Two or more \`[`Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics")\` headers will have the  same effect.

3.       If policy is neither \``same-origin`\`, \``same-site`\`, nor \``cross-origin`\`, then set policy to null.

4.       If policy is null, then switch on embedderPolicyValue:
"[`unsafe-none`](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")"                  Do nothing.   
"[`credentialless`](#concept-request "Resource Timing")"                  Set policy to \``same-origin`\` if:    
5. response’s [request-includes-credentials](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is true, or              
6. forNavigation is true.            
"[`require-corp`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")"           

Set policy to \``same-origin`\`.  

7.       
Switch on policy:  
null          \``cross-origin`\`                  
Return **allowed**.   
\``same-origin`\`                  
If origin is [same origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") with
response’s [URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s    [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), then return
**allowed**.    

Otherwise, return **blocked**.   
\``same-site`\`                  
If all of the following are true    

8.           origin is [schemelessly same site](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") with response’s     [URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource Timing")     

9.           origin’s [scheme](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is "`https`" or     response’s [URL](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [scheme](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is not     "`https`"    


then return **allowed**.    

Otherwise, return **blocked**.    

\``Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-site`\` does not consider a    response delivered via a secure transport to match
a non-secure requesting origin,    even if their hosts are otherwise same site. Securely-transported
responses will only    match a securely-transported initiator.  


To *queue a cross-origin embedder policy CORP violation report*, given a[response](#concept-response "Resource Timing")
response, an [environment settings object](#concept-response "Resource Timing")settingsObject,
a string destination, and a boolean reportOnly,run these steps:


1.       Let endpoint be settingsObject’s [policy container](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing")’s [embedder policy](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")’s [report-only reporting endpoint](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") if reportOnly is true and settingsObject’s [policy container](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [embedder policy](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [reporting endpoint](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") otherwise.

2.       Let serializedURL be the result of [serializing a response URL for reporting](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") with response.

3.       Let disposition be "`reporting`" if reportOnly is true; otherwise "`enforce`".

4.       Let body be a new object containing the following properties:  

|   |
||-----------------|---------------|
| - | -               |
|   |
|   | "`type`"        | "`corp`"      |
|   | "`blockedURL`"  | serializedURL |
|   | "`destination`" | destination   |
|   | "`disposition`" | disposition   |

5.       [Generate and queue a report](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") for settingsObject’s [global object](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") given the ["`coep`" report type](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), endpoint, and body. [[REPORTING]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")


### 3.8. \``Sec-Purpose`\` header

The \`*`Sec-Purpose`*\` HTTP requestheader specifies that the request serves one or more purposes
other than requesting the resource forimmediate use by the user.

The \`[`Sec-Purpose`](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")\` header field is a [structured header](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")whose value must be a [token](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").

The sole [token](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") defined is `prefetch`.
Itindicates the request’s purpose is to fetch a resource that is anticipated to
be needed shortly.

The server can use this to adjust the caching expiry for prefetches, to disallow
theprefetch, or to treat it differently when counting page visits.

## 4. Fetching

The algorithm below defines [fetching](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"). In broad strokes, it takesa [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") and one or more algorithms to run at various points during the operation. A[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") is passed to the last two algorithms listed below. The first two algorithmscan be used to capture uploads.


To *fetch*, given a [request](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing") request,
anoptional algorithm*processRequestBodyChunkLength*, anoptional
algorithm*processRequestEndOfBody*,an optional algorithm *processEarlyHintsResponse*,
an optionalalgorithm *processResponse*, an optionalalgorithm *processResponseEndOfBody*,
an optional algorithm*processResponseConsumeBody*,and an optional boolean *useParallelQueue*
(default false), runthe steps below. If given, processRequestBodyChunkLength must
be an algorithm acceptingan integer representing the number of bytes transmitted.
If given,processRequestEndOfBody must be an algorithm accepting no arguments. If
given,processEarlyHintsResponse must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing"). Ifgiven,
processResponse must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing"). If
given,processResponseEndOfBody must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing"). Ifgiven,
processResponseConsumeBody must be an algorithm accepting a [response](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing")and null, failure, or a [byte sequence](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing").

The user agent may be asked to*suspend* the ongoing fetch.The user agent may either
accept or ignore the suspension request. The suspended fetch can be*resumed*. The
user agent shouldignore the suspension request if the ongoing fetch is updating the
response in the HTTP cache forthe request.

The user agent does not update the entry in the HTTP cache for a [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")if request’s cache mode is "no-store" or a \``Cache-Control:
no-store`\` header appears inthe response. [[HTTP-CACHING]](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")


1.       [Assert](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing"): request’s [mode](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") is "`navigate`" or  processEarlyHintsResponse is null.  

	Processing of early hints ([responses](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") whose [status](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing")  is 103) is only vetted for navigations.

2.       Let taskDestination be null.

3.       Let crossOriginIsolatedCapability be false.

4.       [Populate request from client](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") given request.

5.       If request’s [client](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") is non-null, then:  


1.         Set taskDestination to request’s [client](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")’s   [global object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing").   

2.         Set crossOriginIsolatedCapability to request’s   [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")’s   [cross-origin isolated capability](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing").  


-       If useParallelQueue is true, then set taskDestination to the result of [starting a new parallel queue](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

-       Let timingInfo be a new [fetch timing info](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") whose [start time](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") and [post-redirect start time](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") are the [coarsened shared current time](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") given crossOriginIsolatedCapability, and [render-blocking](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is set to request’s [render-blocking](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

-       Let fetchParams be a new [fetch params](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") whose [request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is request, [timing info](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is timingInfo, [process request body chunk length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is processRequestBodyChunkLength, [process request end-of-body](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is processRequestEndOfBody, [process early hints response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is processEarlyHintsResponse, [process response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is processResponse, [process response consume body](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is processResponseConsumeBody, [process response end-of-body](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") is processResponseEndOfBody, [task destination](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") is taskDestination, and [cross-origin isolated capability](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is crossOriginIsolatedCapability.

-       If request’s [body](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is a [byte sequence](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing"), then set request’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") to request’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") [as a body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing").

-       Run the [WebDriver BiDi clone network request body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") steps with request.

-       If all of the following conditions are true:  
-         request’s [URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [scheme](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") is an   [HTTP(S) scheme](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")   

-         request’s [mode](#biblio-navigation-timing "Resource Timing") is "`same-origin`",   "`cors`", or "`no-cors`"   

-         request’s [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is not null, and request’s   [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s [global object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is a   `[Window](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")` object   

-         request’s [method](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is \``GET`\`   

-         request’s [unsafe-request flag](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is not set or   request’s [header list](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") [is empty](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")  


then:  


1.         [Assert](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy"): request’s [origin](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") is [same origin](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")   with request’s [client](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")’s   [origin](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy").   

2.         Let onPreloadedResponseAvailable be an algorithm that runs the following   step given a [response](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") response: set fetchParams’s   [preloaded response candidate](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy") to response.   

3.         Let foundPreloadedResource be the result of invoking   [consume a preloaded resource](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy") for request’s [client](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy"), given   request’s [URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy"), request’s [destination](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy"),   request’s [mode](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy"), request’s   [credentials mode](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy"), request’s [integrity metadata](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy"),   and onPreloadedResponseAvailable.   

4.         If foundPreloadedResource is true and fetchParams’s   [preloaded response candidate](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") is null, then set fetchParams’s   [preloaded response candidate](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") to "`pending`".  


-       
If request’s [header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy")  [does not contain](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy")
\``Accept`\`, then:  


1.         Let value be \``*/*`\`.   

2.         If request’s [initiator](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") is "`prefetch`", then set   value to the [document \``Accept`\` header value](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy").   

3.         Otherwise, the user agent should set value to the first matching statement, if    any, switching on request’s [destination](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy"):    
"`document`"              "`frame`"              "`iframe`"              the [document \``Accept`\` header value](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy")              "`image`"              \``image/png,image/svg+xml,image/*;q=0.8,*/*;q=0.5`\`              "`json`"              \``application/json,*/*;q=0.5`\`              "`style`"              \``text/css,*/*;q=0.1`\`              "`text`"              \``text/plain,*/*;q=0.5`\`                   
4.         [Append](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy") (\``Accept`\`, value) to   request’s [header list](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy").  


-       
If request’s [header list](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy")  [does not contain](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy")
\``Accept-Language`\` and request’s  [client](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") is non-null:  


1.         Let emulatedLanguage be the [WebDriver BiDi emulated language](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") for   request’s [client](#http-scheme "HTML Standard").   

2.         If emulatedLanguage is non-null:   


1.           Let encodedEmulatedLanguage be emulatedLanguage,    [isomorphic encoded](#biblio-html "HTML Standard").    

2.           [Append](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")    (\``Accept-Language`\`, encodedEmulatedLanguage) to request’s    [header list](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").   


-       
If request’s [header list](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") [does not contain](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")
\``Accept-Language`\`, then user agents should [append](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")
(\``Accept-Language`, an appropriate [header value](#http-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")) to
request’s [header list](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").

-       
If request’s [internal priority](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is null, then use
request’s [priority](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), [initiator](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."),  [destination](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), and [render-blocking](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") in an  [implementation-defined](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") manner to set
request’s  [internal priority](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to an [implementation-defined](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") object.  

The [implementation-defined](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") object could encompass stream weight and  dependency for HTTP/2, priorities used in
*Extensible Prioritization Scheme for HTTP*  for transports where it applies (including
HTTP/3), and equivalent information used to prioritize  dispatch and processing of
HTTP/1 fetches. [[RFC9218]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")

-       
If request is a [subresource request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."):  


1.         Let record be a new [fetch record](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose   [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is request and [controller](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")   is fetchParams’s [controller](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").   

2.         [Append](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") record to request’s   [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [fetch group](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s   [fetch records](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").  


-       
Run [main fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") given
fetchParams.

-       
Return fetchParams’s [controller](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").


To *populate request from client* given a [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")
request:


1.       If request’s [traversable for user prompts](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is "`client`":  


1.         Set request’s [traversable for user prompts](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to   "`no-traversable`".   

2.         If request’s [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is non-null:    


1.           Let global be request’s [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s     [global object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").     

2.           If global is a `[Window](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")` object and global’s     [navigable](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is not null, then set request’s     [traversable for user prompts](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to global’s     [navigable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [traversable navigable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").    


-       If request’s [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is "`client`":  


1.         [Assert](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css): request’s [client](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) is non-null.
