Otherwise, if httpRequest’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") does       [include credentials](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and isAuthenticationFetch is true, set       authorizationValue to httpRequest’s [current URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),       converted to an \``Authorization`\` value.       

4.             If authorizationValue is non-null, then [append](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")       (\``Authorization`\`, authorizationValue) to httpRequest’s       [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").      


-         If there’s a [proxy-authentication entry](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), use it as appropriate.    

	This intentionally does not depend on httpRequest’s    [credentials mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

-         Run the [WebDriver BiDi before request sent](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") steps with request.   

-         Set httpCache to the result of [determining the HTTP cache partition](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),   given httpRequest.   

-         If httpCache is null, then set httpRequest’s   [cache mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to "`no-store`".   

-         If httpRequest’s [cache mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is neither "`no-store`"    nor "`reload`", then:    


1.           Set storedResponse to the result of selecting a response from the      httpCache, possibly needing validation, as per the      "[Constructing Responses from Caches](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")" chapter of *HTTP Caching*, if any.      [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")      

	As mandated by HTTP, this still takes the \``Vary`\`      [header](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") into account.     

2.           If storedResponse is non-null, then:      


1.             If [cache mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`default`", storedResponse        is a [stale-while-revalidate response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), and httpRequest’s        [client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is non-null, then:        


1.               Set response to storedResponse.         

2.               Set response’s [cache state](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle") to "`local`".         

3.               Let revalidateRequest be a [clone](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") of         request.         

4.               Set revalidateRequest’s [cache mode](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues "You can also press the 'y' key") set to         "`no-cache`".         

5.               Set revalidateRequest’s         [prevent no-cache cache-control header modification flag](/commit-snapshots/394d20d144ed1401c2c0e02c35bc3608cb2a2269/ "You can also press the 'y' key").         

6.               Set revalidateRequest’s [service-workers mode](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese") set to         "`none`".         

7.               [In parallel](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese"), run [main fetch](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese") given a new [fetch params](https://ko.htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Korean") whose          [request](ko "Content Security Policy Level 3") is revalidateRequest.          

	This fetch is only meant to update the state of httpCache          and the response will be unused until another cache access. The stale response will be used          as the response to current request. This fetch is issued in the context of a client so if          it goes away the request will be terminated.        


-             Otherwise:        


1.               If storedResponse is a [stale response](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then set the         revalidatingFlag.         

2.               If the revalidatingFlag is set and httpRequest’s          [cache mode](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") is neither "`force-cache`" nor          "`only-if-cached`", then:          


1.                 If storedResponse’s [header list](#statuses "Content Security Policy Level 3")           [contains](#bodies "Content Security Policy Level 3") \``ETag`\`, then           [append](#requests "Content Security Policy Level 3") (\``If-None-Match`\`, \``ETag`\`'s           [value](#miscellaneous "Content Security Policy Level 3")) to httpRequest’s [header list](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3").           

2.                 If storedResponse’s [header list](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3")           [contains](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3") \``Last-Modified`\`, then           [append](#network-partition-keys "Content Security Policy Level 3") (\``If-Modified-Since`\`,           \``Last-Modified`\`'s [value](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3")) to httpRequest’s           [header list](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3").          


See also the "[Sending a Validation Request](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3")" chapter of          *HTTP Caching*. [[HTTP-CACHING]](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3")         

-               Otherwise, set response to storedResponse and set         response’s [cache state](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") to "`local`".        


-       
[If aborted](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then return the [appropriate network error](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3") for
fetchParams.

-       
If response is not null, then run the [WebDriver BiDi response started](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3") steps with
request and response.

-       
If response is null, then:  


1.         If httpRequest’s [cache mode](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   "`only-if-cached`", then return a [network error](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3").   

2.         Let forwardResponse be the result of running [HTTP-network fetch](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3") given   httpFetchParams, includeCredentials, and isNewConnectionFetch.   

3.         If httpRequest’s [method](#content-type-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") is [unsafe](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") and   forwardResponse’s [status](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff? "Content Security Policy Level 3") is in the range 200 to 399, inclusive,   invalidate appropriate stored responses in httpCache, as per the   "[Invalidating Stored Responses](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")" chapter of *HTTP Caching*, and set   storedResponse to null. [[HTTP-CACHING]](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")   

4.         If the revalidatingFlag is set and forwardResponse’s    [status](#fetching "Content Security Policy Level 3") is 304, then:    


1.           Update storedResponse’s [header list](#override-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") using      forwardResponse’s [header list](#scheme-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"), as per the      "[Freshening Stored Responses upon Validation](#http-redirect-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")" chapter of *HTTP Caching*.      [[HTTP-CACHING]](#http-network-or-cache-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")      

	This updates the stored response in cache as well.     

2.           Set response to storedResponse.     

3.           Set response’s [cache state](#cors-preflight-cache "Content Security Policy Level 3") to "`validated`".    


-         If response is null, then:    


1.           Set response to forwardResponse.     

2.           Store httpRequest and forwardResponse in httpCache, as per      the "[Storing Responses in Caches](#deferred-fetch-quota "Content Security Policy Level 3")" chapter of *HTTP Caching*.      [[HTTP-CACHING]](#fetch-api "Content Security Policy Level 3")      

	If forwardResponse is a [network error](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3"), this effectively caches      the network error, which is sometimes known as "negative caching".      

	The associated [body info](#request-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") is stored in the cache      alongside the response.    


-       
Set response’s [URL list](#fetch-method "Content Security Policy Level 3") to a [clone](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3") of
httpRequest’s [URL list](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3").

-       
If httpRequest’s [header list](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3") [contains](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3")
\``Range`\`, then set response’s [range-requested flag](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3").

-       
Set response’s [request-includes-credentials](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level 3") to
includeCredentials.

-       
If response’s [status](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3") is 401, httpRequest’s
[response tainting](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not "`cors`",
includeCredentials is  true, and request’s [traversable for user prompts](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a  [traversable navigable](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3"):  


1.         Needs testing: multiple \``WWW-Authenticate`\` headers, missing,   parsing issues.   

2.         If request’s [body](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") is non-null, then:    


1.           If request’s [body](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [source](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") is null,     then return a [network error](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3").     

2.           Set request’s [body](#informative "Content Security Policy Level 3") to the [body](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3")     of the result of [safely extracting](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") request’s     [body](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [source](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3").    


-         If request’s [use-URL-credentials flag](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") is unset or    isAuthenticationFetch is true, then:    


1.           If fetchParams is [canceled](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly"), then return the     [appropriate network error](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Mixed Content") for fetchParams.     

2.           Let username and password be the result of prompting the end user     for a username and password, respectively, in request’s     [traversable for user prompts](#http-origin "The Web Origin Concept").     

3.           [Set the username](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") given request’s [current URL](#preface "Infra Standard") and     username.     

4.           [Set the password](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") given request’s [current URL](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") and     password.    


-         Set response to the result of running [HTTP-network-or-cache fetch](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") given   fetchParams and true.  


-       
If response’s [status](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is 407, then:  


1.         If request’s [traversable for user prompts](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") is   "`no-traversable`", then return a [network error](#infrastructure "Infra Standard").   

2.         Needs testing: multiple \``Proxy-Authenticate`\` headers, missing,   parsing issues.   

3.         If fetchParams is [canceled](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF"), then return the   [appropriate network error](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") for fetchParams.   

4.         Prompt the end user as appropriate in request’s    [traversable for user prompts](#biblio-html "HTML Standard") and store the result as a    [proxy-authentication entry](#biblio-mimesniff "MIME Sniffing Standard"). [[HTTP]](#biblio-streams "Streams Standard")    

	Remaining details surrounding proxy authentication are defined by HTTP.   

5.         Set response to the result of running [HTTP-network-or-cache fetch](#biblio-webtransport "WebSockets Standard") given   fetchParams.  


-       
If all of the following are true  

-         response’s [status](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF") is 421   

-         isNewConnectionFetch is false   

-         request’s [body](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") is null, or request’s   [body](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") is non-null and request’s [body](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism")’s   [source](#biblio-tls "The Transport Layer Security (TLS) Protocol Version 1.3") is non-null  


then:  


1.         If fetchParams is [canceled](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), then return the   [appropriate network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") for fetchParams.   

2.         Set response to the result of running [HTTP-network-or-cache fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") given   fetchParams, isAuthenticationFetch, and true.  


-       
If isAuthenticationFetch is true, then create an [authentication entry](#concept-request "Resource Timing") for
request and the given realm.

-       
Return response. Typically response’s [body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")’s [stream](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is still being enqueued to after returning.


### 4.7. HTTP-network fetch


To *HTTP-network fetch*, given a [fetch params](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource
Timing")fetchParams, an optional boolean includeCredentials (default false), and
anoptional boolean forceNewConnection (default false), run these steps:


1.       Let request be fetchParams’s [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").

2.       If request’s [client](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") [is offline](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), then return a [network error](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").

3.       Let response be null.

4.       Let timingInfo be fetchParams’s [timing info](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").

5.       Let networkPartitionKey be the result of [determining the network partition key](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") given request.

6.       Let newConnection be "`yes`" if forceNewConnection is true; otherwise "`no`".

7.       Switch on request’s [mode](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"):  
"`websocket`"                  Let connection be the result of   [obtaining a WebSocket connection](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), given   request’s [current URL](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").   
"`webtransport`"                  Let connection be the result of [obtaining a WebTransport connection](#concept-response "Resource Timing"),   given networkPartitionKey and request.   
Otherwise                  Let connection be the result of   [obtaining a connection](#concept-response "Resource Timing"), given networkPartitionKey,   request’s [current URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), includeCredentials, and   newConnection.  

8.       Run these steps, but [abort when](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") fetchParams is  [canceled](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing"):  


1.         If connection is failure, then return a [network error](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").   

2.         Set timingInfo’s [final connection timing info](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") to   the result of calling [clamp and coarsen connection timing info](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") with   connection’s [timing info](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), timingInfo’s   [post-redirect start time](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"), and fetchParams’s   [cross-origin isolated capability](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").   

3.         If connection is an HTTP/1.x connection, request’s   [body](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") is non-null, and request’s [body](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")’s   [source](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") is null, then return a [network error](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").   

4. Set timingInfo’s [final network-request start time](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")   to the [coarsened shared current time](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") given fetchParams’s   [cross-origin isolated capability](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").          
5.         Set response to the result of making an HTTP request over connection    using request with the following caveats:    
6.           Follow the relevant requirements from HTTP. [[HTTP]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") [[HTTP-CACHING]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")     

7.           If request’s [body](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") is non-null, and request’s      [body](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing")’s [source](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is null, then the user agent may have a      buffer of up to 64 kibibytes and store a part of request’s [body](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")      in that buffer. If the user agent reads from request’s [body](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")      beyond that buffer’s size and the user agent needs to resend request, then instead      return a [network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing").      


The resending is needed when the connection is timed out, for example.       

	The buffer is not needed when request’s [body](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing")’s       [source](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is non-null, because request’s [body](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") can       be recreated from it.       

	When request’s [body](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [source](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is null, it       means [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") is created from a `[ReadableStream](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing")` object, which means       [body](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing") cannot be recreated and that is why the buffer is needed.      

8.           While true:      


1.             Set timingInfo’s       [final network-response start time](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to the       [coarsened shared current time](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") given fetchParams’s       [cross-origin isolated capability](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing"), immediately after the user       agent’s HTTP parser receives the first byte of the response (e.g., frame header bytes for       HTTP/2 or response status line for HTTP/1.x).       

2.             Wait until all the HTTP response headers are transmitted.       

3.             Run the [WebDriver BiDi response started](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") steps with       request and response.       

4.             Let status be the HTTP response’s status code.       

5.             If status is in the range 100 to 199, inclusive:        


1.               If timingInfo’s         [first interim network-response start time](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing") is 0, then set         timingInfo’s         [first interim network-response start time](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") to         timingInfo’s [final network-response start time](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing").         

2.               If request’s [mode](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing") is "`websocket`" and         status is 101, then [break](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing").         

3.               If status is 103 and fetchParams’s         [process early hints response](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing") is non-null, then         [queue a fetch task](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") to run fetchParams’s         [process early hints response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing"), with [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").         

4.               [Continue](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").        


These kind of HTTP responses are eventually followed by a "final" HTTP        response.       

-             [Break](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").      


The exact layering between Fetch and HTTP still needs to be sorted through and    therefore response represents both a [response](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") and    an HTTP response here.    

	If the HTTP request results in a TLS client certificate dialog, then:    


1.           If request’s [traversable for user prompts](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") is a     [traversable navigable](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing"), then make the dialog available in request’s     [traversable for user prompts](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing").     

2.           Otherwise, return a [network error](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing").    


To transmit request’s [body](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") body, run these steps:    


1.           If body is null and fetchParams’s     [process request end-of-body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") is non-null, then     [queue a fetch task](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") given fetchParams’s     [process request end-of-body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") and fetchParams’s     [task destination](#biblio-navigation-timing "Resource Timing").     

2.           Otherwise, if body is non-null:      


1.             Let processBodyChunk given bytes be these steps:        


1.               If fetchParams is [canceled](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), then abort these         steps.         

2.               Run this step [in parallel](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"): transmit bytes.         

3.               If fetchParams’s         [process request body chunk length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is non-null, then run         fetchParams’s [process request body chunk length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") given         bytes’s [length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").        


-             Let processEndOfBody be these steps:        


1.               If fetchParams is [canceled](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), then abort these         steps.         

2.               If fetchParams’s [process request end-of-body](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is         non-null, then run fetchParams’s         [process request end-of-body](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").        


-             Let processBodyError given e be these steps:        


1.               If fetchParams is [canceled](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), then abort these         steps.         

2.               If e is an "`[AbortError](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing")`" `[DOMException](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing")`,         then [abort](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") fetchParams’s         [controller](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing").         

3.               Otherwise, [terminate](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") fetchParams’s         [controller](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing").        


-             [Incrementally read](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") request’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") given       processBodyChunk, processEndOfBody, processBodyError, and       fetchParams’s [task destination](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing").      


-       
[If aborted](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), then:  


1.         If connection uses HTTP/2, then transmit an `RST_STREAM` frame.   

2.         Return the [appropriate network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") for fetchParams.  


-       
Let buffer be an empty [byte sequence](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing").  

This represents an internal buffer inside the network layer of the user agent.

-       
Let stream be a [new](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") `[ReadableStream](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer
Policy")`.

-       
Let pullAlgorithm be the following steps:  


1.         Let promise be [a new promise](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy").   

2.         Run the following steps [in parallel](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"):    


1.           If the size of buffer is smaller than a lower limit chosen by the user agent and the     ongoing fetch is [suspended](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"), [resume](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") the fetch.     

2.           Wait until buffer is not empty.     

3.           [Queue a fetch task](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") to run the following steps, with fetchParams’s      [task destination](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy").      


1.             [Pull from bytes](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") buffer into stream.       

2.             If stream is [errored](#fetch-timing-info-post-redirect-start-time "Referrer Policy"), then [terminate](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy")       fetchParams’s [controller](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy").       

3.             [Resolve](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy") promise with undefined.      


-         Return promise.  


-       
Let cancelAlgorithm be an algorithm that [aborts](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy")
fetchParams’s [controller](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") with
reason, given reason.

-       
[Set up](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy")
stream with byte reading support with [pullAlgorithm](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy")
set to pullAlgorithm, [cancelAlgorithm](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy")
set to cancelAlgorithm.

-       
Set response’s [body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") to a new [body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") whose [stream](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") is
stream.

-       
Run the [WebDriver BiDi clone network response body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#networking-task-source "Referrer Policy") steps with
request and response.

-       
[![(This is a tracking vector.)](idl "Referrer Policy")](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy")
If includeCredentials is true, then the user agent should [parse and store response \``Set-Cookie`\`
headers](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") given
request and response.

-       
Run these steps [in parallel](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy"):  


1.         Run these steps, but [abort when](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") fetchParams is    [canceled](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy"):    


1.           While true:      


1.             If one or more bytes have been transmitted from response’s message body, then:        


1.               Let bytes be the transmitted bytes.         

2.               Let codings be the result of [extracting header list values](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy") given         \``Content-Encoding`\` and response’s [header list](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy").         

3.               Let filteredCoding be "`@unknown`".         

4.               If codings is null or failure, then set filteredCoding to         the empty string.         

5.               Otherwise, if codings’s [size](#http-scheme "Referrer Policy") is greater than 1, then set         filteredCoding to "`multiple`".         

6.               Otherwise, if codings[0] is the empty string, or it is supported by the         user agent, and is a [byte-case-insensitive](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") match for an entry listed in the         *HTTP Content Coding Registry*, then set filteredCoding to the result         of [byte-lowercasing](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") codings[0]. [[IANA-HTTP-PARAMS]](#biblio-html "HTML Standard")         

7.               Set response’s [body info](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s         [content encoding](#concept-fetch "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to filteredCoding.         

8.               Increase response’s [body info](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s         [encoded size](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") by bytes’s         [length](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").         

9.               Set bytes to the result of [handling content          codings](#http-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") given codings and bytes.          

	This makes the \``Content-Length`\` [header](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") unreliable          to the extent that it was reliable to begin with.         

10.               Increase response’s [body info](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s         [decoded size](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") by         bytes’s [length](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").         

11.               If bytes is failure, then [terminate](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")         fetchParams’s [controller](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").         

12.               Append bytes to buffer.         

13.               If the size of buffer is larger than an upper limit chosen by the user agent, ask         the user agent to [suspend](#http-tab-or-space-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") the ongoing fetch.        


-             Otherwise, if the bytes transmission for response’s message body is done       normally and stream is [readable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then       [close](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") stream, and abort these in-parallel steps.      


-         [If aborted](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then:    


1.           If fetchParams is [aborted](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then:      


1.             Set response’s [aborted flag](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").       

2.             If stream is [readable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then       [error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") stream with the result of       [deserialize a serialized abort reason](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") given fetchParams’s       [controller](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [serialized abort reason](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")       and an [implementation-defined](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") [realm](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").      


-           Otherwise, if stream is [readable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."),     [error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") stream with a `[TypeError](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")`.     

-           If connection uses HTTP/2, then transmit an `RST_STREAM` frame.     

-           Otherwise, the user agent should close connection unless it would be bad for      performance to do so.      

	For instance, the user agent could keep the connection open if it knows there’s      only a few bytes of transfer remaining on a reusable connection. In this case it could be      worse to close the connection and go through the handshake process again for the next fetch.    


These are run [in parallel](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") as at this point it is unclear whether
response’s [body](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is relevant
(response might be a  redirect).

-       
Return response. Typically response’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [stream](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is still being enqueued to after returning.


### 4.8. CORS-preflight fetch

This is effectively the user agent implementation of the check to see if the[CORS protocol](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) is understood. The so-called [CORS-preflight request](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css "subtitle"). If successful itpopulates the [CORS-preflight cache](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle") to minimize the number of these[fetches](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle").


To *CORS-preflight fetch*, given a [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")request,
run these steps:


1.       Let preflight be a new [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose  [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is \``OPTIONS`\`,  [URL list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is a [clone](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of request’s  [URL list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),  [initiator](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is request’s [initiator](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),  [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is request’s [destination](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),  [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is request’s [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),  [referrer](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is request’s [referrer](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),  [referrer policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is request’s [referrer policy](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"),  [mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`cors`",  [response tainting](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is "`cors`", and  [WebDriver id](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is request’s [WebDriver id](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").  

	The [service-workers mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of preflight does not matter  as this algorithm uses [HTTP-network-or-cache fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") rather than [HTTP fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

2.       [Append](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") (\``Accept`\`, \``*/*`\`) to preflight’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

3.       [Append](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") (\`[`Access-Control-Request-Method`](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")\`, request’s [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")) to preflight’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

4.       Let headers be the [CORS-unsafe request-header names](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with request’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").

5.       If headers [is not empty](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then:  


1.         Let value be the items in headers separated from each other by   \``,`\`.   

2.         [Append](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")   (\`[`Access-Control-Request-Headers`](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")\`, value) to   preflight’s [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").  


This intentionally does not use [combine](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), as 0x20 following  0x2C is not the way this was implemented, for better or worse.

-       Let response be the result of running [HTTP-network-or-cache fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given a new [fetch params](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is preflight.

-       If a [CORS check](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for request and response returns success and  response’s [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is an [ok status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then:  

	The [CORS check](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is done on request rather than preflight  to ensure the correct [credentials mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is used.  


1.         Let methods be the result of [extracting header list values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given   \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Methods`](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")\` and response’s   [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

2.         Let headerNames be the result of [extracting header list values](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given   \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Headers`](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")\` and response’s   [header list](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

3.         If either methods or headerNames is failure,   return a [network error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

4.         If methods is null and request’s [use-CORS-preflight flag](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")    is set, then set methods to a new list containing request’s    [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").    

	This ensures that a [CORS-preflight fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") that happened due to    request’s [use-CORS-preflight flag](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") being set is    [cached](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   

5.         If request’s [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not in methods,   request’s [method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [CORS-safelisted method](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), and   request’s [credentials mode](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle") is "`include`" or   methods does not contain \``*`\`, then return a [network error](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key").   

6.         If one of request’s [header list](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new/choose "You can also press the 'y' key")’s   [names](https://whatwg.org/chat "You can also press the 'y' key") is a [CORS non-wildcard request-header name](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key") and is not a   [byte-case-insensitive](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") match for an [item](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese") in headerNames, then   return a [network error](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese").   

7.         [For each](ja "Simplified Chinese") unsafeName of the   [CORS-unsafe request-header names](zh-hans "Korean") with request’s   [header list](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3"), if unsafeName is not a [byte-case-insensitive](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3")   match for an [item](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") in headerNames and request’s   [credentials mode](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`include`" or headerNames does not   contain \``*`\`, return a [network error](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3").   

8.         Let max-age be the result of [extracting header list values](#http "Content Security Policy Level 3") given   \`[`Access-Control-Max-Age`](#methods "Content Security Policy Level 3")\` and response’s   [header list](#statuses "Content Security Policy Level 3").   

9.         If max-age is failure or null, then set max-age to 5.   

10.         If max-age is greater than an imposed limit on   [max-age](#responses "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then set max-age to the imposed limit.   

11.         If the user agent does not provide for a [cache](#authentication-entries "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then   return response.   

12.         For each method in methods for which there is a   [method cache entry match](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3") using request, set matching entry’s   [max-age](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3") to max-age.   

13.         For each method in methods for which there is no   [method cache entry match](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3") using request, [create a new cache entry](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3") with   request, max-age, method, and null.   

14.         For each headerName in headerNames for which there is a   [header-name cache entry match](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3") using request, set matching entry’s   [max-age](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3") to max-age.   

15.         For each headerName in headerNames for which there is no   [header-name cache entry match](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") using request, [create a new cache entry](#cookie-infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3")   with request, max-age, null, and headerName.   

16.         Return response.  


-       Otherwise, return a [network error](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3").


### 4.9. CORS-preflight cache

A user agent has an associated [CORS-preflight cache](#http-responses "Content Security Policy Level 3").
A*CORS-preflight cache* is a [list](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") of [cache entries](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3").

A *cache entry* consists of:

- *key* (a [network partition key](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"))     
- *byte-serialized origin* (a [byte sequence](#cross-origin-resource-policy-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"))     
- *URL* (a [URL](#sec-purpose-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"))     
- *max-age* (a number of seconds)     
- *credentials* (a boolean)     
- *method* (null, \``*`\`, or a [method](#http-network-or-cache-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"))     
- *header name* (null, \``*`\`, or a [header name](#cors-preflight-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3"))   

[Cache entries](#cors-preflight-cache "Content Security Policy Level 3") must be removed after the seconds specified in their[max-age](#cors-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") field have passed since storing the entry. [Cache entries](#tao-check "Content Security Policy Level 3") maybe removed before that moment arrives.


To *create a new cache entry*, given request,max-age, method, and headerName, run
these steps:


1.       Let entry be a [cache entry](#headers-class "Content Security Policy Level 3"), initialized as follows:  
[key](#body-mixin "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  The result of [determining the network partition key](#request-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") given   request   
[byte-serialized origin](#fetch-method "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  The result of [byte-serializing a request origin](#garbage-collection "Content Security Policy Level 3") with request   
[URL](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  request’s [current URL](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")   
[max-age](#atomic-http-redirect-handling "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  max-age   
[credentials](#basic-safe-cors-protocol-setup "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  True if request’s [credentials mode](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level 3") is   "`include`", and false otherwise   
[method](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  method   
[header name](#fetch-elsewhere-request "Content Security Policy Level 3")                  headerName  

2.       [Append](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") entry to the user agent’s [CORS-preflight cache](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3").


To *clear cache entries*, given a request,[remove](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") any [cache entries](#index-defined-here "Content Security Policy Level 3") in the user agent’s [CORS-preflight cache](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3")whose [key](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") is the result of[determining the network partition key](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3") given
request,[byte-serialized origin](#informative "Content Security Policy Level 3") is the result of[byte-serializing a request origin](#idl-index "Content Security Policy Level 3") with
request, and [URL](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")is request’s
[current URL](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3").

There is a *cache entry match* for a [cache entry](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request
Headers")entry with request if entry’s [key](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly") is theresult of [determining the network partition key](#biblio-mix "Mixed Content") given
request,entry’s [byte-serialized origin](#biblio-referrer "Upgrade Insecure Requests") is the result of[byte-serializing a request origin](#biblio-referrer "Referrer Policy") with
request, entry’s[URL](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") is request’s
[current URL](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept"), and one of

- entry’s [credentials](#preface "Infra Standard") is true     
- entry’s [credentials](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is false and request’s [credentials mode](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") is not "`include`".   

is true.

There is a *method cache entry match* formethod using request when there is a [cache entry](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") in the user agent’s[CORS-preflight cache](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") for which there is a [cache entry match](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") with
requestand its [method](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") is method or \``*`\`.

There is a *header-name cache entry match* forheaderName using request when there
is a [cache entry](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") in the useragent’s [CORS-preflight cache](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF") for which there is a [cache entry match](#biblio-encoding "Encoding Standard")
withrequest and one of

- its [header name](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics") is a [byte-case-insensitive](#biblio-streams "MIME Sniffing Standard") match for headerName     
- its [header name](#biblio-webidl "Web IDL Standard") is \``*`\` and headerName is not a [CORS non-wildcard request-header name](#biblio-webtransport "WebTransport")   

is true.

### 4.10. CORS check


To perform a *CORS check* for a request andresponse, run these steps:


1.       Let origin be the result of [getting](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#biblio-tls "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism")\` from response’s [header list](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics").

2.       If origin is null, then return failure.  

	Null is not \``null`\`.

3.       If request’s [credentials mode](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") is not "`include`" and origin is \``*`\`, then return success.

4.       If the result of [byte-serializing a request origin](#concept-request "Resource Timing") with request is not origin, then return failure.

5.       If request’s [credentials mode](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is not "`include`", then return success.

6.       Let credentials be the result of [getting](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")\` from response’s [header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing").

7.       If credentials is \``true`\`, then return success.

8.       Return failure.


### 4.11. TAO check


To perform a *TAO check* for a request andresponse, run these steps:


1.       [Assert](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing"): request’s [origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is not "`client`".

2.       If request’s [timing allow failed flag](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is set, then return failure.

3.       Let values be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Resource Timing") \``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` from response’s [header list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").

4.       If values [contains](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") "`*`", then return success.

5.       If values [contains](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") the result of [serializing a request origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") with request, then return success.

6.       If request’s [mode](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is "`navigate`" and  request’s [current URL](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [origin](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is not  [same origin](#concept-response "Resource Timing") with request’s [origin](#concept-response "Resource Timing"), then return failure.  

	This is necessary for navigations of a nested navigable. There,  request’s [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") would be the container document’s  [origin](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") and the [TAO check](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") would return failure. Since navigation timing  never validates the results of the [TAO check](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing"), the nested document would still have access  to the full timing information, but the container document would not.

7.       If request’s [response tainting](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is "`basic`", then return success.

8.       Return failure.


To *append to a request’s navigation timing allow values list*, given a[request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
request and a [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
response:


1.       [Assert](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing"): request is a [navigation request](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing").

2.       Let taoValues be the result of [getting, decoding, and splitting](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") \``Timing-Allow-Origin`\` from response’s [header list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").

3.       If taoValues is null, then set taoValues to « ».

4.       [Append](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") taoValues to request’s [navigation timing allow values list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").


To perform a *navigation TAO check* for a[response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
response and an [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")destinationOrigin:


1.       [For each](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") taoValues of response’s  [navigation timing allow values list](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing"):  


1.         If taoValues [contains](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") "`*`", then   [continue](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").   

2.         If taoValues [contains](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") destinationOrigin,   [serialized](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing"), then [continue](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing").   

3.         Return [failure](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing").  


-       Return [success](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").


### 4.12. Deferred fetching

Deferred fetching allows callers to request that a fetch is invoked at the latest
possiblemoment, i.e., when a [fetch group](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is [terminated](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing"), or after atimeout.

The *deferred fetch task source* is a [task source](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing") used to update the result of adeferred fetch. User agents must prioritize tasks in this [task source](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") before other tasksources, specifically task sources that can result in running scripts such as the[DOM manipulation task source](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), to reflect the most recent state of a[`fetchLater()`](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") call before running any scripts that might depend on it.


To *queue a deferred fetch* given a [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")
request, a null or`[DOMHighResTimeStamp](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")`
activateAfter, and onActivatedWithoutTermination,which is an algorithm that takes
no arguments:


1.       [Populate request from client](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") given request.

2.       Set request’s [service-workers mode](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing") to "`none`".

3.       Set request’s [keepalive](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") to true.

4.       Let deferredRecord be a new [deferred fetch record](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") whose [request](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") is request, and whose [notify invoked](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing") is onActivatedWithoutTermination.

5.       [Append](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing") deferredRecord to request’s [client](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing")’s [fetch group](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing")’s [deferred fetch records](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing").

6.       If activateAfter is non-null, then run the following steps [in parallel](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"):  


1.         The user agent should wait until any of the following conditions is met:    
2.           At least activateAfter milliseconds have passed.     

3.           The user agent has a reason to believe that it is about to lose the opportunity to     execute scripts, e.g., when the browser is moved to the background, or when     request’s [client](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s     [global object](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") is a `[Window](#fetch-params "Resource Timing")` object whose     [associated document](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") had a "`hidden`" [visibility state](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing") for     a long period of time.    


4.         [Process](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") deferredRecord.  


-       
Return deferredRecord.


To compute the *total request length* of a [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")
request:


1.       Let totalRequestLength be the [length](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") of request’s [URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing"), [serialized](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") with [*exclude fragment*](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing") set to true.

2.       Increment totalRequestLength by the [length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") of request’s [referrer](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), [serialized](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

3.       [For each](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") (name, value) of request’s [header list](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), increment totalRequestLength by name’s [length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") + value’s [length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

4.       Increment totalRequestLength by request’s [body](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [length](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

5.       Return totalRequestLength.


To *process deferred fetches* given a [fetch group](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")
fetchGroup:


1.       [For each](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") [deferred fetch record](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") deferredRecord of fetchGroup’s [deferred fetch records](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), [process a deferred fetch](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") deferredRecord.


To *process a deferred fetch* deferredRecord:


1.       If deferredRecord’s [invoke state](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is not "`pending`", then return.

2.       Set deferredRecord’s [invoke state](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") to "`sent`".

3.       [Fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") deferredRecord’s [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing").

4.       [Queue a global task](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") on the [deferred fetch task source](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") with deferredRecord’s [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [global object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") to run deferredRecord’s [notify invoked](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing").


#### 4.12.1. Deferred fetching quota

*This section is non-normative.*

The deferred-fetch quota is allocated to a [top-level traversable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") (a "tab"),amounting to 640 kibibytes. The top-level document and its same-origin directly nested documents canuse this quota to queue deferred fetches, or delegate some of it to cross-origin nested documents,using permissions policy.

By default, 128 kibibytes out of these 640 kibibytes are allocated to delegating
the quota tocross-origin nested documents, each reserving 8 kibibytes.

The top-level [document](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"), and subsequently its nested documents, can control how muchof their quota is delegated to cross-origin child documents, using permissions policy. By default,the "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer
Policy")`" policy is enabled for any origin, while"`[deferred-fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer
Policy")`" is enabled for the top-level document’s origin only. Byrelaxing the
"`[deferred-fetch](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")`" policy for particular origins and nesteddocuments,
the top-level document can allocate 64 kibibytes to those nested documents. Similarly,
byrestricting the "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")`" policy for a particular
origin ornested document, the document can prevent the document from reserving the
8 kibibytes it wouldreceive by default. By disabling the "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer
Policy")`" policy for thetop-level document itself, the entire 128 kibibytes delegated
quota is collected back into the mainpool of 640 kibibytes.

Out of the allocated quota for a [document](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy"), only 64 kibibytes can be usedconcurrently for the same reporting origin (the [request](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy")’s [URL](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy")’s[origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy")). This prevents a situation where particular third-party libraries would reservequota opportunistically, before they have data to send.


Any of the following calls to [`fetchLater()`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy") would throw due to the request itself exceeding the 64 kibibytes quota allocated to a reporting origin. Note that the size of the request includes the [URL](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy") itself, the [body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy"), the [header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy"), and the [referrer](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy").

```algorithm
fetchLater(a_72_kb_url);
fetchLater("https://origin.example.com", {headers: headers_exceeding_64kb});
fetchLater(a_32_kb_url, {headers: headers_exceeding_32kb});
fetchLater("https://origin.example.com", {method: "POST", body: body_exceeding_64_kb});
fetchLater(a_62_kb_url /* with a 3kb referrer */);
```

In the following sequence, the first two requests would succeed, but the third one
would throw. That’s because the overall 640 kibibytes quota was not exceeded in
the first two calls, however the 3rd request exceeds the reporting-origin quota for
`https://a.example.com`, and would throw.

```dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
fetchLater("https://a.example.com", {method: "POST", body: a_64kb_body});
fetchLater("https://b.example.com", {method: "POST", body: a_64kb_body});
fetchLater("https://a.example.com");
```

Same-origin nested documents share the quota of their parent. However, cross-origin
or cross-agent iframes only receive 8kb of quota by default. So in the following
example, the first three calls would succeed and the last one would throw.

```self-link
// In main page
fetchLater("https://a.example.com", {method: "POST", body: a_64kb_body});

// In same-origin nested document
fetchLater("https://b.example.com", {method: "POST", body: a_64kb_body});

// In cross-origin nested document at https://fratop.example.com
fetchLater("https://a.example.com", {body: a_5kb_body});
fetchLater("https://a.example.com", {body: a_12kb_body});
```

To make the previous example not throw, the top-level document can delegate some
of its quota to `https://fratop.example.com`, for example by serving the following header:

```dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
Permissions-Policy: deferred-fetch=(self "https://fratop.example.com")```
    
Each nested document reserves its own quota. So the following would work, because 
each frame reserves 8 kibibytes: 

```note
// In cross-origin nested document at https://fratop.example.com/frame-1
fetchLater("https://a.example.com", {body: a_6kb_body});

// In cross-origin nested document at https://fratop.example.com/frame-2
fetchLater("https://a.example.com", {body: a_6kb_body});
```
    
The following tree illustrates how quota is distributed to different nested documents 
in a tree: 
    
     -       `https://top.example.com`, with permissions policy set to   `Permissions-policy: deferred-fetch=(self "https://ok.example.com")`   
             -         `https://top.example.com/frame`: shares quota with the top-level traversable, as     they are same origin.     
                 -           `https://x.example.com`: receives 8 kibibytes.
        
       
-         
`https://x.example.com`: receives 8 kibibytes.     
        
         -           `https://top.example.com`: 0. Even though it’s same origin with the     top-level traversable, it does not automatically share its quota as they are separated by a     cross-origin intermediary.
        
       
-         
`https://ok.example.com/good`: receives 64 kibibytes, granted via the     "`[deferred-fetch](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by
default.")`" policy.     
        
         -           `https://x.example.com`: receives no quota. Only documents with the same     origin as the top-level traversable can grant the 8 kibibytes based on the     "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")`" policy.
        
       
-         
`https://ok.example.com/redirect`, navigated to    `https://x.example.com`: receives no quota. The reserved 64 kibibytes for    `https://ok.example.com` are 
not available for    `https://x.example.com`.    
       
-         
`https://ok.example.com/back`, navigated to    `https://top.example.com`: shares quota with the top-level traversable, as they’re 
   same origin.   
      
    
    
In the above example, the [top-level traversable](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and its [same origin](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") descendants share a quota of 384 kibibytes. That value is computed as such: 
    
     -       640 kibibytes are initially granted to the [top-level traversable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").  
     
-       128 kibibytes are reserved for the "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")`" policy.  
     
-       64 kibibytes are reserved for the container navigating to  `https://ok.example/good`.  
     
-       64 kibibytes are reserved for the container navigating to  `https://ok.example/redirect`, and lost when it navigates away.  
     
- `https://ok.example.com/back` did not reserve 64 kibibytes, because it navigated  back to [top-level traversable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s origin.       
-       640 − 128 − 64 − 64 = 384 kibibytes. 
    
      
This specification defines a [policy-controlled feature](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") identified by the
string"*`deferred-fetch`*". Its[default allowlist](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
"`self`".
   
This specification defines a [policy-controlled feature](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") identified by the
string"*`deferred-fetch-minimal`*". Its[default allowlist](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
"`*`".
   
The *quota reserved for `deferred-fetch-minimal`* is 128 kibibytes.
   
Each [navigable container](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") has an associated
number*reserved deferred-fetch quota*. Its possible values are*minimal quota*, which 
is 8 kibibytes, and*normal quota*, which is 0 or 64 kibibytes. Unlessstated otherwise, 
it is 0.
   

    
To get the *available deferred-fetch quota* given a [document](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by
default.")document and an [origin](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")-or-null
origin:
   

     1.       Let controlDocument be document’s [deferred-fetch control document](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css). 
     
2.       Let navigable be controlDocument’s [node navigable](https://resources.whatwg.org/spec.css "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Let isTopLevel be true if controlDocument’s [node navigable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is a [top-level traversable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"); otherwise false. 
     
4.       Let deferredFetchAllowed be true if controlDocument is [allowed to use](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") the [policy-controlled feature](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") "`[deferred-fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`"; otherwise false. 
     
5.       Let deferredFetchMinimalAllowed be true if controlDocument is [allowed to use](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") the [policy-controlled feature](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`"; otherwise false. 
     
6.       Let quota be the result of the first matching statement:  
             isTopLevel is true and deferredFetchAllowed is false          0          isTopLevel is true and deferredFetchMinimalAllowed is false                  640 kibibytes    
        
	640kb should be enough for everyone.   
       isTopLevel is true                  512 kibibytes    
        
	The default of 640 kibibytes, decremented by    [quota reserved for `deferred-fetch-minimal`](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   
       deferredFetchAllowed is true, and navigable’s   [navigable container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [reserved deferred-fetch quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is   [normal quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")          [normal quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")          deferredFetchMinimalAllowed is true, and navigable’s   [navigable container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [reserved deferred-fetch quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is   [minimal quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")          [minimal quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")          Otherwise          0             
7.       Let quotaForRequestOrigin be 64 kibibytes. 
     
8.       [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") navigable in controlDocument’s  [node navigable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [inclusive descendant navigables](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") whose  [active document](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [deferred-fetch control document](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is  controlDocument:  
     

       1.         [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") container in navigable’s   [active document](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [shadow-including inclusive descendants](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") which is a   [navigable container](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), decrement quota by container’s   [reserved deferred-fetch quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").   
       
2.         [For each](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [deferred fetch record](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") deferredRecord of    navigable’s [active document](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [relevant settings object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s    [fetch group](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s    [deferred fetch records](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"):    
       

         1.           If deferredRecord’s [invoke state](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")     is not "`pending`", then [continue](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").     
         
2.           Let requestLength be the [total request length](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") of     deferredRecord’s [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").     
         
3.           Decrement quota by requestLength.     
         
4.           If deferredRecord’s [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s     [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is [same origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with origin,     then decrement quotaForRequestOrigin by requestLength.    
        

      

     
-       If quota is equal or less than 0, then return 0. 
     
-       If quota is less than quotaForRequestOrigin, then return quota. 
     
-       Return quotaForRequestOrigin.
    

      

    
To *reserve deferred-fetch quota* for a [navigable container](https://whatwg.org/
"subtitle")container given an [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") originToNavigateTo:
    
This is called on navigation, when the source document of the navigation is the[navigable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s parent document. It potentially reserves either 64kb or 8kb of quota forthe container and its navigable, if allowed by permissions policy. It is not observable to thecontainer document whether the reserved quota was used in practice. This algorithm assumes that thecontainer’s document might delegate quota to the navigated container, and the reserved quota wouldonly apply in that case, and would be ignored if it ends up being shared. If quota was reserved andthe document ends up being [same origin](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/issues/new/choose "You can also press the 'y' key") with its parent, the quota would be[freed](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch/commits "You can also press the 'y' key").
   

     1.       Set container’s [reserved deferred-fetch quota](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/tree/master/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") to 0. 
     
2.       Let controlDocument be container’s [node document](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/labels/fetch "Japanese")’s [deferred-fetch control document](https://triple-underscore.github.io/Fetch-ja.html "Japanese"). 
     
3.       If the [inherited policy](https://htmlspecs.com/fetch/ "Simplified Chinese") for "`[deferred-fetch](ko "Content Security Policy Level 3")`", container and originToNavigateTo is `"Enabled"`, and the [available deferred-fetch quota](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3") for controlDocument is equal or greater than [normal quota](#goals "Content Security Policy Level 3"), then set container’s [reserved deferred-fetch quota](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3") to [normal quota](#infrastructure "Content Security Policy Level 3") and return. 
     
4.       If all of the following conditions are true:  
             5.         controlDocument’s [node navigable](#methods "Content Security Policy Level 3") is a [top-level traversable](#terminology-headers "Content Security Policy Level 3");   
       
6.         the [inherited policy](#bodies "Content Security Policy Level 3")   for "`[deferred-fetch-minimal](#responses "Content Security Policy Level 3")`", container and   originToNavigateTo is `"Enabled"`; and   
       
7.         the [size](#fetch-groups "Content Security Policy Level 3") of controlDocument’s [node navigable](#resolving-domains "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s   [descendant navigables](#connections "Content Security Policy Level 3"), [removing](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3") any [navigable](#port-blocking "Content Security Policy Level 3")   whose [navigable container](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [reserved deferred-fetch quota](#http-extensions "Content Security Policy Level 3") is not   [minimal quota](#cookies "Content Security Policy Level 3"), is less than   [quota reserved for `deferred-fetch-minimal`](#cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") /   [minimal quota](#set-cookie-header "Content Security Policy Level 3"),  
      
      
then set container’s [reserved deferred-fetch quota](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") to  [minimal quota](#http-cors-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3").
    

      

    
To *potentially free deferred-fetch quota* for a [document](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level
3")document, if document’s [node navigable](#cors-protocol-and-credentials "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [container document](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3") isnot null, and its [origin](#cors-protocol-exceptions "Content Security Policy Level 3") is [same origin](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") with
document, thenset document’s [node navigable](#x-content-type-options-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [navigable container](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-nosniff?
