A `[Response](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object has an associated*response*
(a[response](#acknowledgments "Content Security Policy Level 3")).

A `[Response](#ipr "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object also has an associated *headers*
(null or a`[Headers](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object),
initially null.

A `[Response](#references "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object’s [body](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3") is its[response](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s [body](#informative "Content Security Policy Level 3").

---
`response = new [Response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")(body = null [, init])`          
Creates a `[Response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3")` whose body is body,
and status, status message, and headers are provided by init.
`response = [Response](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") . [error](#biblio-fetch-metadata "Fetch Metadata Request Headers")()`

Creates network error `[Response](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly")`.
`response = [Response](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Mixed Content") . [redirect](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests")(url, status = 302)`

Creates a redirect `[Response](#http-origin "The Web Origin Concept")` that redirects to
url with status status.
`response = [Response](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") . [json](#preface "The Web Origin Concept")(data [, init])`

Creates a `[Response](#preface "Infra Standard")` whose body is the JSON-encoded data, and
status, status message, and headers are provided by init.
`response . [type](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard")`          
Returns response’s type, e.g., "`cors`".
`response . [url](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard")`          
Returns response’s URL, if it has one; otherwise the empty string.
`response . [redirected](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard")`          
Returns whether response was obtained through a redirect.
`response . [status](#infrastructure "Infra Standard")`          
Returns response’s status.
`response . [ok](#biblio-infra "Infra Standard")`          
Returns whether response’s status is an [ok status](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF").
`response . [statusText](#biblio-abnf "Augmented BNF for Syntax Specifications: ABNF")`          
Returns response’s status message.
`response . [headers](#biblio-http "HTML Standard")`          
Returns response’s headers as `[Headers](#biblio-streams "Streams Standard")`.
`response . [clone](#biblio-webidl "URL Standard")()`          
Returns a clone of response.

---


To *create* a `[Response](#biblio-rfc7405 "Case-Sensitive String Support in ABNF")` object,
given a[response](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") response,
[headers guard](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") guard,
and[realm](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") realm,
run these steps:


1.       Let responseObject be a [new](#biblio-tls "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") `[Response](#biblio-http "The Transport Layer Security (TLS) Protocol Version 1.3")` object with realm.

2.       Set responseObject’s [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") to response.

3.       Set responseObject’s [headers](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") to a [new](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") `[Headers](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")` object with realm, whose [headers list](#concept-request "Resource Timing") is response’s [headers list](#concept-request "Resource Timing") and [guard](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is guard.

4.       Return responseObject.


To *initialize a response*, given a `[Response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")`
object response,`[ResponseInit](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")`
init, and null or a [body with type](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
body:


1.       If init["`[status](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")`"] is not in the range 200 to 599, inclusive, then [throw](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") a `[RangeError](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")`.

2.       If init["`[statusText](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")`"] is not the empty string and does not match the [reason-phrase](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") token production, then [throw](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") a `[TypeError](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")`.

3.       Set response’s [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [status](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to init["`[status](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")`"].

4.       Set response’s [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [status message](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to init["`[statusText](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")`"].

5.       If init["`[headers](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")`"] [exists](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing"), then [fill](#concept-response "Resource Timing") response’s [headers](#concept-response "Resource Timing") with init["`[headers](#concept-response "Resource Timing")`"].

6.       If body is non-null, then:  


1.         If response’s [status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") is a [null body status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), then    [throw](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing") a `[TypeError](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing")`.    

	101 and 103 are included in [null body status](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") due to their use elsewhere.    They do not affect this step.   

2.         Set response’s [body](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") to body’s   [body](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing").   

3.         If body’s [type](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") is non-null and   response’s [header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") [does not contain](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")   \``Content-Type`\`, then [append](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") (\``Content-Type`\`,   body’s [type](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")) to response’s   [header list](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing").  


---


The*`new Response(body, init)`*constructor steps are:


1.       Set [this](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to a new [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").

2.       Set [this](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [headers](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to a [new](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") `[Headers](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")` object with [this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")’s [relevant realm](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing"), whose [header list](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") is [this](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [header list](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") and [guard](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is "`response`".

3.       Let bodyWithType be null.

4.       If body is non-null, then set bodyWithType to the result of [extracting](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") body.

5.       Perform [initialize a response](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing") given [this](#fetch-controller-next-manual-redirect-steps "Resource Timing"), init, and bodyWithType.


The static *`error()`* method steps are to return theresult of [creating](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") a `[Response](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")`
object, given a new [network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing"),"`immutable`",
and the [current realm](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing").


The static*`redirect(url, status)`* method stepsare:


1.       Let parsedURL be the result of [parsing](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") url with [current settings object](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [API base URL](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing").

2.       If parsedURL is failure, then [throw](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing") a `[TypeError](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing")`.

3.       If status is not a [redirect status](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing"), then [throw](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") a `[RangeError](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")`.

4.       Let responseObject be the result of [creating](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing") a `[Response](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing")` object, given a new [response](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing"), "`immutable`", and the [current realm](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing").

5.       Set responseObject’s [response](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")’s [status](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") to status.

6.       Let value be parsedURL, [serialized](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing") and [isomorphic encoded](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing").

7.       [Append](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing") (\``Location`\`, value) to responseObject’s [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing")’s [header list](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing").

8.       Return responseObject.


The static*`json(data, init)`* method stepsare:


1.       Let bytes the result of running [serialize a JavaScript value to JSON bytes](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") on data.

2.       Let body be the result of [extracting](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") bytes.

3.       Let responseObject be the result of [creating](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") a `[Response](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing")` object, given a new [response](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing"), "`response`", and the [current realm](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing").

4.       Perform [initialize a response](#fetch-params "Resource Timing") given responseObject, init, and (body, "`application/json`").

5.       Return responseObject.


The *`type`* getter steps are to return [this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [type](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing").

The *`url`* getter steps are to returnthe empty string if [this](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing")’s [response](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing")’s [URL](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is null;otherwise [this](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [URL](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"),[serialized](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") with
[*exclude fragment*](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") setto true.

The *`redirected`* getter steps are to return true if[this](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [URL list](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [size](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") isgreater than 1; otherwise false.

To filter out [responses](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") that are the result of aredirect, do this directly through the API, e.g., `fetch(url, { redirect:"error"
})`.This way a potentially unsafe [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") cannot accidentally leak.

The *`status`* getter steps are to return[this](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [status](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing").

The *`ok`* getter steps are to return true if[this](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [response](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [status](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") is an [ok status](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing");otherwise false.

The *`statusText`* getter steps are to return[this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing")’s [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing")’s [status message](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing").

The *`headers`* getter steps are to return[this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")’s [headers](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing").

---


The *`clone()`* method steps are:


1.       If [this](#biblio-navigation-timing "Resource Timing") is [unusable](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Navigation Timing"), then [throw](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") a `[TypeError](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")`.

2.       Let clonedResponse be the result of [cloning](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") [this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy").

3.       Return the result of [creating](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") a `[Response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")` object, given clonedResponse, [this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s [headers](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s [guard](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy"), and [this](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy")’s [relevant realm](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy").


### 5.6. Fetch methods

```partial interface mixin [WindowOrWorkerGlobalScope](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") { 
[[NewObject](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy")] [Promise](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer
Policy")<[Response](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy")> [fetch](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer
Policy")([RequestInfo](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy") *algorithm
input*, optional [RequestInit](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") *algorithm
init* = {});};dictionary *algorithm
DeferredRequestInit* : [RequestInit](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") { 
[DOMHighResTimeStamp](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") *algorithm
activateAfter*;};[[Exposed](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer
Policy")=Window]interface *dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
FetchLaterResult* {  readonly attribute [boolean](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy")
[activated](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer
Policy");};partial interface [Window](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") { 
[[NewObject](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy"), [SecureContext](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy")]
[FetchLaterResult](#url "Referrer Policy") [fetchLater](#url "Referrer Policy")([RequestInfo](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy") *note
input*, optional [DeferredRequestInit](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy") *dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
init* = {});};```
   

    
The*`fetch(input, init)`*method steps are:
   

     1.       Let p be [a new promise](#http-scheme "HTML Standard"). 
     
2.       Let requestObject be the result of invoking the initial value of `[Request](#biblio-html "HTML Standard")` as constructor with input and init as arguments. If this throws an exception, [reject](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") p with it and return p. 
     
3.       Let request be requestObject’s [request](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). 
     
4.       If requestObject’s [signal](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is [aborted](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."),  then:  
     

       1.         [Abort the `fetch()` call](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") with p, request, null, and   requestObject’s [signal](#http-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [abort reason](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").   
       
2.         Return p.  
      

     
- Let globalObject be  request’s [client](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [global object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").      
- If globalObject is a `[ServiceWorkerGlobalScope](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")` object, then set request’s [service-workers mode](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to "`none`".      
-       Let responseObject be null. 
     
-       Let relevantRealm be [this](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [relevant realm](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). 
     
-       Let locallyAborted be false.  
      
	This lets us reject promises with predictable timing, when the request to abort  comes from the same thread as the call to fetch. 
     
-       Let controller be null. 
     
-       [Add the following abort steps](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") to requestObject’s  [signal](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."):  
     

       1.         Set locallyAborted to true.   
       
2.         [Assert](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."): controller is non-null.   
       
3.         [Abort](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") controller with requestObject’s   [signal](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [abort reason](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").   
       
4.         [Abort the `fetch()` call](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") with p, request,   responseObject, and requestObject’s [signal](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s   [abort reason](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").  
      

     
-       
      
	Set controller to the result of calling [fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") given  request and [*processResponse*](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") given response being  these steps:  
     

       1.         If locallyAborted is true, then abort these steps.   
       
2.         If response’s [aborted flag](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is set, then:    
       

         1.           Let deserializedError be the result of     [deserialize a serialized abort reason](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") given controller’s     [serialized abort reason](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and relevantRealm.     
         
2.           [Abort the `fetch()` call](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") with p, request,     responseObject, and deserializedError.     
         
3.           Abort these steps.    
        

       
-         If response is a [network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then [reject](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") p   with a `[TypeError](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")` and abort these steps.   
       
-         Set responseObject to the result of [creating](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") a `[Response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")`   object, given response, "`immutable`", and relevantRealm.   
       
-         [Resolve](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") p with responseObject.  
      

     
-       Return p.
    

      

    
To *abort a `fetch()` call*with a promise, request, responseObject, and an error:
   

     1.       [Reject](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) promise with error.  
      
	This is a no-op if promise has already fulfilled. 
     
2.       If request’s [body](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") is non-null and is [readable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then [cancel](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") request’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with error. 
     
3.       If responseObject is null, then return. 
     
4.       Let response be responseObject’s [response](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
5.       If response’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is non-null and is [readable](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then [error](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") response’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with error.
    

      
A `[FetchLaterResult](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` has an associated *activated getter steps*,which 
is an algorithm returning a boolean.
   

    
The *`activated`* getter steps are to returnthe result of running [this](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [activated getter steps](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle").
      

    
The *`fetchLater(input, init)`*method steps are:
   

     1.       Let requestObject be the result of invoking the initial value of `[Request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` as constructor with input and init as arguments. 
     
2.       If requestObject’s [signal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is [aborted](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw [signal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [abort reason](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
3.       Let request be requestObject’s [request](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
4.       Let activateAfter be null. 
     
5.       If init is given and init["`[activateAfter](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`"] [exists](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then set activateAfter to init["`[activateAfter](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`"]. 
     
6.       If activateAfter is less than 0, then throw a `[RangeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`. 
     
7.       If [this](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [relevant global object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [associated document](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not [fully active](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`. 
     
8.       If request’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not an [HTTP(S) scheme](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`. 
     
9.       If request’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not a [potentially trustworthy URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`. 
     
10.       If request’s [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is not null, and request’s  [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") [length](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is null, then throw a `[TypeError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")`.  
      
	Requests whose [body](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is a `[ReadableStream](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` object cannot be  deferred. 
     
11.       Let quota be the [available deferred-fetch quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given request’s [client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and request’s [URL](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [origin](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
12.       Let requested be request’s [total request length](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). 
     
13.       If quota is less than requested, then [throw](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") a `[QuotaExceededError](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")` whose [quota](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is quota and [requested](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is requested. 
     
14.       Let activated be false. 
     
15.       Let deferredRecord be the result of calling [queue a deferred fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") given request, activateAfter, and the following step: set activated to true. 
     
16.       [Add the following abort steps](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") to requestObject’s [signal](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"): Set deferredRecord’s [invoke state](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "subtitle") to "`aborted`". 
     
17.       Return a new `[FetchLaterResult](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key")` whose [activated getter steps](https://github.com/whatwg/fetch "You can also press the 'y' key") are to return activated.
    

      

         
The following call would queue a request to be fetched when the document is terminated: 

```p-summary
fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
method: "POST",
body: JSON.stringify(myReport),
headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" }
})
```
    
The following call would also queue this request after 5 seconds, and the returned 
value would allow callers to observe if it was indeed activated. Note that the request 
is guaranteed to be invoked, even in cases where the user agent throttles timers. 

```p-summary
const result = fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
method: "POST",
body: JSON.stringify(myReport),
headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
activateAfter: 5000
});

function check_if_fetched() {
return result.activated;
}
```
    
The `[FetchLaterResult](#preface "Content Security Policy Level 3")` object can be used together 
with an `[AbortSignal](#url "Content Security Policy Level 3")`. For example: 

```content
let accumulated_events = [];
let previous_result = null;
const abort_signal = new AbortSignal();
function accumulate_event(event) {
if (previous_result) {
if (previous_result.activated) {
// The request is already activated, we can start from scratch.
accumulated_events = [];
} else {
// Abort this request, and start a new one with all the events.
signal.abort();
}
}

accumulated_events.push(event);
result = fetchLater("https://report.example.com", {
method: "POST",
body: JSON.stringify(accumulated_events),
headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
activateAfter: 5000,
abort_signal
});
}
```
    
Any of the following calls to [`fetchLater()`](#http-cache-partitions "Content Security Policy Level 3") would throw: 

```secno
// Only [potentially trustworthy URL](#should-response-to-request-be-blocked-due-to-mime-type? "Content Security Policy Level 3")s are supported.
fetchLater("http://untrusted.example.com");

// The length of the deferred request has to be known when.
fetchLater("https://origin.example.com", {body: someDynamicStream});

// Deferred fetching only works on active windows.
const detachedWindow = iframe.contentWindow;
iframe.remove();
detachedWindow.fetchLater("https://origin.example.com");
```
    
See [deferred fetch quota examples](#origin-header "Content Security Policy Level 3") for examples portraying how the deferred-fetch quota works.
      
### 5.7. Garbage collection
   
The user agent may [terminate](#general "Content Security Policy Level 3") an ongoing fetch if that terminationis not observable through script.
   
"Observable through script" means observable through[`fetch()`](#http-new-header-syntax "Content Security Policy Level 3")’s arguments and return value. Other ways, such as communicatingwith the server through a side-channel are not included.
   
The server being able to observe garbage collection has precedent, e.g., with`[WebSocket](#cors-protocol-examples "Content Security Policy Level 3")` 
and `[XMLHttpRequest](#content-length-header "Content Security Policy Level 3")` objects.
   

         
The user agent can terminate the fetch because the termination cannot be observed. 

```content
fetch("https://www.example.com/")
```
    
The user agent cannot terminate the fetch because the termination can be observed 
through the promise. 

```secno
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/")
```
    
The user agent can terminate the fetch because the associated body is not observable. 

```secno
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/").then(res => res.headers)
```
    
The user agent can terminate the fetch because the termination cannot be observed. 

```content
fetch("https://www.example.com/").then(res => res.body.getReader().closed)
```
    
The user agent cannot terminate the fetch because one can observe the termination 
by registering a handler for the promise object. 

```secno
window.promise = fetch("https://www.example.com/")
.then(res => res.body.getReader().closed)
```
    
The user agent cannot terminate the fetch as termination would be observable via 
the registered handler. 

```secno
fetch("https://www.example.com/")
.then(res => {
res.body.getReader().closed.then(() => console.log("stream closed!"))
})
```
    
(The above examples of non-observability assume that built-in properties and functions, 
such as  `[body.getReader()](#deferred-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3")`, have not been overwritten.)
      
## 6. `data:` URLs
   
For an informative description of `data:` URLs, see RFC 2397. This section replacesthat 
RFC’s normative processing requirements to be compatible with deployed content. 
[[RFC2397]](#bodyinit-unions "Content Security Policy Level 3")
   
A *`data:` URL struct* is a [struct](#response-class "Content Security Policy Level 3") that consists of
a*MIME type* (a [MIME type](#data-urls "Content Security Policy Level 3")) and a*body* 
(a [byte sequence](#http-header-layer-division "Content Security Policy Level 3")).
   

    
The *`data:` URL processor* takes a [URL](#cors-protocol-and-http-caches "Content Security Policy Level
3")dataURL and then runs these steps:
   

     1.       [Assert](#websocket-protocol "Content Security Policy Level 3"): dataURL’s [scheme](#fetch-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3") is "`data`". 
     
2.       Let input be the result of running the [URL serializer](#fetch-elsewhere-fetch "Content Security Policy Level 3") on dataURL with [*exclude fragment*](#fetch-elsewhere-ongoing "Content Security Policy Level 3") set to true. 
     
3.       Remove the leading "`data:`" from input. 
     
4.       Let position point at the start of input. 
     
5.       Let mimeType be the result of [collecting a sequence of code points](#index "Content Security Policy Level 3") that are not equal to U+002C (,), given position. 
     
6.       [Strip leading and trailing ASCII whitespace](#index-defined-elsewhere "Content Security Policy Level 3") from mimeType.  
      
	This will only remove U+0020 SPACE [code points](#normative "Content Security Policy Level 3"), if any. 
     
7.       If position is past the end of input, then return failure. 
     
8.       Advance position by 1. 
     
9.       Let encodedBody be the remainder of input. 
     
10.       Let body be the [percent-decoding](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") of encodedBody. 
     
11.       If mimeType ends with U+003B (;), followed by zero or more U+0020 SPACE, followed by  an [ASCII case-insensitive](#biblio-csp "Content Security Policy Level 3") match for "`base64`", then:  
     

       1.         Let stringBody be the [isomorphic decode](#biblio-sw "Service Workers Nightly") of body.   
       
2.         Set body to the [forgiving-base64 decode](#biblio-upgrade-insecure-requests "Upgrade Insecure Requests") of stringBody.   
       
3.         If body is failure, then return failure.   
       
4.         Remove the last 6 [code points](#http-origin "The Web Origin Concept") from mimeType.   
       
5.         Remove trailing U+0020 SPACE [code points](#biblio-origin "The Web Origin Concept") from mimeType, if any.   
       
6.         Remove the last U+003B (;) from mimeType.  
      

     
-       If mimeType [starts with](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") "`;`", then prepend "`text/plain`" to mimeType. 
     
-       Let mimeTypeRecord be the result of [parsing](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") mimeType. 
     
-       If mimeTypeRecord is failure, then set mimeTypeRecord to `text/plain;charset=US-ASCII`. 
     
-       Return a new [`data:` URL struct](#dom-global-fetch "Infra Standard") whose [MIME type](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") is mimeTypeRecord and [body](#infrastructure "Infra Standard") is body.
    

      
## Background reading
   
*This section and its subsections are informative only.*
   
### HTTP header layer division
   
For the purposes of fetching, there is an API layer (HTML’s `img`, CSS’s`background-image`), early 
fetch layer, service worker layer, and network & cachelayer. \``Accept`\` and \``Accept-Language`\` 
are set in the early fetch layer(typically by the user agent). Most other headers 
controlled by the user agent, such as\``Accept-Encoding`\`, \``Host`\`, and \``Referer`\`, are set in thenetwork 
& cache layer. Developers can set headers either at the API layer or in the serviceworker 
layer (typically through a `[Request](#biblio-websockets "WebSockets Standard")` object). 
Developers have almost no control over[forbidden request-headers](#biblio-webtransport "WebTransport"), but can control
\``Accept`\` and have the means toconstrain and omit \``Referer`\` for instance.
   
### Atomic HTTP redirect handling
   
Redirects (a [response](#authentication-entry "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") whose [status](#biblio-cookies "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism") or[internal response](#biblio-tls "Cookies: HTTP State Management Mechanism")’s (if any) [status](#biblio-http "HTTP Semantics") is a[redirect status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing")) are not exposed to APIs. Exposing redirects might leak information nototherwise available through a cross-site scripting attack.
   
A fetch to `https://example.org/auth` that includes a`Cookie` marked `HttpOnly` could result in a redirect to`https://other-origin.invalid/4af955781ea1c84a3b11`. This new 
URL contains asecret. If we expose redirects that secret would be available through 
a cross-sitescripting attack.
   
### Basic safe CORS protocol setup
   
For resources where data is protected through IP authentication or a firewall(unfortunately 
relatively common still), using the [CORS protocol](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
is**unsafe**. (This is the reason why the [CORS protocol](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") had to beinvented.)
   
However, otherwise using the following [header](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")
is**safe**:

```dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *
```
   
Even if a resource exposes additional information based on cookie or HTTPauthentication, 
using the above [header](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") will not revealit. It will share the resource with APIs such as`[XMLHttpRequest](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource
Timing")`, much like it is already shared with`curl` and `wget`.
   
Thus in other words, if a resource cannot be accessed from a random device connected 
tothe web using `curl` and `wget` the aforementioned[header](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") is not to be included. If it can be accessedhowever, it is perfectly fine to do so.
   
### CORS protocol and HTTP caches
   
If [CORS protocol](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") requirements are more complicated than setting\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing")\` to `*` 
or a static[origin](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), \``Vary`\` is to be used.[[HTML]](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") [[HTTP]](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") [[HTTP-CACHING]](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")

```dfn-paneled has-dfn-panel
Vary: Origin
```
   
In particular, consider what happens if \``Vary`\` is *not* used and a server isconfigured 
to send \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")\` for a certainresource only in response to a [CORS request](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing"). When a user agent receives a response to anon-[CORS request](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") for that resource (for example, as the result of a [navigationrequest](#concept-response "Resource Timing")), the response will lack \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#concept-response "Resource Timing")\`and the user agent will cache that response. Then, if the user agent subsequently encounters a[CORS request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") for the resource, it will use that cached response from the previousnon-[CORS request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing"), without \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#concept-fetch "Resource Timing")\`.
   
But if \``Vary: Origin`\` is used in the same scenario described above, it will causethe user 
agent to [fetch](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") a response that includes\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")\`, rather than using the cached responsefrom the previous non-[CORS request](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing") that lacks\`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")\`.
   
However, if \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](#fetch-timing-info "Resource Timing")\` is set to`*` or a static 
[origin](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Resource Timing") for a particular resource, then configure the serverto always send \`[`Access-Control-Allow-Origin`](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")\` in responses for theresource — for non-[CORS requests](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") as well as [CORSrequests](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") — and do not use
\``Vary`\`.
   
### WebSockets
   
As part of establishing a connection, the `[WebSocket](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")` 
object initiates a special kind of[fetch](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") (using a [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") whose [mode](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")
is"`websocket`") which allows it to share in many fetch policy decisions, such*HTTP 
Strict Transport Security (HSTS)*. Ultimately this results in fetch calling into*WebSockets* 
to obtain a dedicated connection. [[WEBSOCKETS]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")[[HSTS]](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing")
   
Fetch used to define[obtain a WebSocket connection](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") and[establish a WebSocket connection](#fetch-controller-report-timing-steps "Resource Timing") directly, butboth are now defined in
*WebSockets*. [[WEBSOCKETS]](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")
   
## Using fetch in other standards
   
In its essence [fetching](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is an exchange of a [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") for a[response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing"). In reality it is rather complex mechanism for standards to adopt and usecorrectly. This section aims to give some advice.
   
Always ask domain experts for review.
   
This is a work in progress.
   
### Setting up a request
   
The first step in [fetching](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is to create a [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), and populate its[items](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").
   
Start by setting the [request](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Resource Timing") and [method](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing"),as defined by HTTP. If your
\``POST`\` or \``PUT`\` [request](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing") needs abody, you set [request](#fetch-controller-full-timing-info "Resource Timing")’s [body](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") to a [byte sequence](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"), or toa new [body](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") whose [stream](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing") is a `[ReadableStream](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing")` 
you created. [[HTTP]](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing")
   
Choose your [request](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing")’s [destination](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing") using the guidance in the[destination table](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing"). [Destinations](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing")
affect*Content Security Policy* and have other implications such as the \``[Sec-Fetch-Dest](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource
Timing")`\`header, so they are much more than informative metadata. If a new feature 
requires a[destination](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structuredserialize "Resource Timing") that’s not in the [destination table](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing"),please[file an issue](#fetch-controller-serialized-abort-reason "Resource Timing")to discuss. [[CSP]](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")
   
Set your [request](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing")’s [client](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") to the[environment settings object](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#sec-list-and-record-specification-type "Resource Timing") you’re operating in. Web-exposed APIs are generally defined withWeb IDL, for which every object that implements an [interface](https://tc39.es/ecma262/#realm "Resource Timing") has a[relevant settings object](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#aborterror "Resource Timing") you can use. For example, a [request](https://webidl.spec.whatwg.org/#idl-DOMException "Resource Timing") associated with an[element](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") would set the [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing")’s [client](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/structured-data.html#structureddeserialize "Resource Timing") to the element’s[node document](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")’s [relevant settings object](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing"). All features that are directly web-exposedby JavaScript, HTML, CSS, or other `[Document](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing")` 
subresources should have a[client](#fetch-controller "Resource Timing").
   
If your [fetching](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") is not directly web-exposed, e.g., it is sent in the backgroundwithout relying on a current `[Window](#fetch-params "Resource Timing")` 
or `[Worker](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing")`, leave [request](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing")’s[client](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") as null and set the [request](#fetch-params "Resource Timing")’s [origin](#fetch-params "Resource Timing"),[policy container](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing"), [service-workers mode](#fetch-params-controller "Resource Timing"), and[referrer](#fetch-controller-state "Resource Timing") to appropriate values instead, e.g., by copying them from the[environment settings object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") ahead of time. In these more advanced cases, make sure thedetails of how your fetch handles
*Content Security Policy* and[referrer policy](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") are fleshed out. Also make sure you handle concurrency, as callbacks(see [Invoking fetch and processing responses](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing")) would be posted on a [parallel queue](#biblio-navigation-timing "Navigation Timing"). [[REFERRER]](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") [[CSP]](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")
   
Think through the way you intend to handle cross-origin resources. Some features 
may only work inthe [same origin](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), in which case set your [request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [mode](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")
to"`same-origin`". Otherwise, new web-exposed features should almost always set 
their[mode](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") to "`cors`". 
If your feature is not web-exposed, or you thinkthere is another reason for it to 
fetch cross-origin resources without CORS, please[file an issue](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")to discuss.
   
For cross-origin requests, also determines if [credentials](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") are to be included withthe requests, in which case set your [request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")’s [credentials mode](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")
to"`include`".
   
Figure out if your fetch needs to be reported to *Resource Timing*, and with which[initiator type](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"). By passing an [initiator type](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") to the[request](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing"), reporting to
*Resource Timing* will be done automatically once thefetch is done and the [response](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing") is fully downloaded. [[RESOURCE-TIMING]](https://w3c.github.io/hr-time/#dom-domhighrestimestamp "Resource Timing")
   
If your request requires additional HTTP headers, set its [header list](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") toa [header list](#connection-timing-info "Resource Timing") that contains those headers, e.g., «
(\``My-Header-Name`\`,\``My-Header-Value`\`) ». Sending custom headers may have implications, 
such as requiring a[CORS-preflight fetch](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing"), so handle with care.
   
If you want to override the default caching mechanism, e.g., disable caching for 
this[request](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing"), set the request’s [cache mode](https://w3c.github.io/ServiceWorker/#service-worker-timing-info "Resource Timing") to a value other
than"`default`".
   
Determine whether you want your request to support redirects. If you don’t, set 
its[redirect mode](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#list "Resource Timing") to "`error`".
   
Browse through the rest of the parameters for [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") to see if something else isrelevant to you. The rest of the parameters are used less frequently, often for special purposes,and they are documented in detail in the [§ 2.2.5 Requests](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") section of this standard.
   
### Invoking fetch and processing responses
   
Aside from a [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct "Resource Timing") the [fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#struct-item "Resource Timing") operation takes several optionalarguments. For those arguments that take an algorithm: the algorithm will be called from a task (orin a [parallel queue](#biblio-resource-timing "Resource Timing") if
[*useParallelQueue*](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is true).
   
Once the [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is set up, to determine which algorithms to pass to[fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"), determine how you would like to process the [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"), and inparticular at what stage you would like to receive a callback:
       Upon completion          
This is how most callers handle a [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy"), for example  [scripts](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") and [style resources](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy").  The [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy")’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-string "Referrer Policy") is read in its entirety into a  [byte sequence](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy"), and then processed by the caller.  
     
To process a [response](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") upon completion, pass an algorithm as the 
[*processResponseConsumeBody*](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy") argument of [fetch](#fetch-timing-info "Referrer Policy"). The given  algorithm is passed a [response](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy") and an argument representing the fully read  [body](#fetch-timing-info-post-redirect-start-time "Referrer Policy") (of the [response](#fetch-timing-info-start-time "Referrer Policy")’s  [internal response](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy")). The second argument’s values have the following  meaning:  
           null         The [response](#fetch-timing-info-end-time "Referrer Policy")’s [body](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy") is null, due to the response being a   [network error](#fetch-timing-info-render-blocking "Referrer Policy") or having a [null body status](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy").        
failure         Attempting to [fully read](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy") the contents of the [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#global-object "Referrer Policy")’s   [body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy") failed, e.g., due to an I/O error.        
a [byte sequence](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy")          
      
[Fully reading](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#parallel-queue "Referrer Policy") the contents of the [response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy")’s    [internal response](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/infrastructure.html#enqueue-the-following-steps "Referrer Policy")’s [body](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") succeeded.    
       
A [byte sequence](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#queue-a-global-task "Referrer Policy") containing the full contents will be passed also for a    [request](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") whose [mode](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/webappapis.html#environment-settings-object "Referrer Policy") is
"`no-cors`". Callers have to    be careful when handling such content, as it should 
not be accessible to the requesting    [origin](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy"). For example, the caller may use contents of a
"`no-cors`"    [response](https://w3c.github.io/webdriver-bidi/#webdriver-bidi-network-is-offline "Referrer Policy") to display image contents directly to the user, but those image contents    should not be directly exposed to scripts in the embedding document.  
          

              

       1.         Let request be a [request](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") whose [URL](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") is    `https://stuff.example.com/` and [client](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy") is [this](https://github.com/whatwg/infra/issues/201 "Referrer Policy")’s    [relevant settings object](#url "Referrer Policy").    
       
2.         [Fetch](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy") request, with     [*processResponseConsumeBody*](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url "Referrer Policy") set to the following steps given     a [response](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy") response and null, failure, or a [byte sequence](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-url-scheme "Referrer Policy")     contents:     
       

         1.           If contents is null or failure, then present an error to the user.      
         
2.           Otherwise, parse contents considering the metadata from response,      and perform your own operations on it.     
        

      

         Headers first, then chunk-by-chunk          
In some cases, for example when playing video or progressively loading images, callers 
might  want to stream the response, and process it one chunk at a time. The [response](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") is  handed over to the fetch caller once the headers are processed, and the caller  continues from there.  
     
To process a [response](#http-scheme "HTML Standard") chunk-by-chunk, pass an algorithm to the 
[*processResponse*](#fetch-scheme "HTML Standard") argument of [fetch](#biblio-html "HTML Standard"). The given  algorithm is passed a [response](#http "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") when the response’s headers have been  received and is responsible for reading the [response](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s  [body](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [stream](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") in order to download the rest  of the response. For convenience, you may also pass an algorithm to the 
[*processResponseEndOfBody*](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") argument, which is called once you have finished  fully reading the response and its [body](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). Note that unlike 
[*processResponseConsumeBody*](#http-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), passing the 
[*processResponse*](https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/#mime-type "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") or
[*processResponseEndOfBody*](#http-tab-or-space "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") arguments  does not guarantee that the response will be fully read, and callers are responsible to  read it themselves.  
     
The [*processResponse*](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#ascii-whitespace "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") argument is also useful for handling the  [response](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [header list](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") and [status](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") without  handling the [body](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") at all. This is used, for example, when handling responses  that do not have an [ok status](#http-newline-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").  
     

              

       1.         Let request be a [request](#http-tab-or-space-byte "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose [URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is    `https://stream.example.com/` and [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is [this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s    [relevant settings object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").    
       
2.         [Fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") request, with     [*processResponse*](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") set to the following steps given a     [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") response:     
       

         1.           If response is a [network error](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), then present an error to the user.      
         
2.           Otherwise, if response’s [status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#collect-a-sequence-of-code-points "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is not an      [ok status](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), present some fallback value to the user.      
         
3.           Otherwise, [get a reader](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") for [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s      [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [stream](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."), and process in an appropriate way for the      MIME type identified by [extracting a MIME type](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") from response’s [headers list](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.").     
        

      

         Ignore the response          
In some cases, there is no need for a [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") at all, e.g., in the case of  `[navigator.sendBeacon()](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by
default.")`. Processing a response and passing callbacks to  [fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#assert "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is optional, so omitting the callback would [fetch](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#iteration-break "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") without  expecting a response. In such cases, the [response](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s [body](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s  [stream](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#code-point "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") will be discarded, and the caller does not have to worry about downloading  the contents unnecessarily.  
     
[Fetch](#example-http-quoted-string "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") a [request](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") whose  [URL](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
`https://fire-and-forget.example.com/`,  [method](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is
\``POST`\`, and [client](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.") is [this](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default.")’s  [relevant settings object](https://infra.spec.whatwg.org/#string-position-variable "Multiple vendors' web servers enable HTTP TRACE method by default."). 
      
Apart from the callbacks to handle responses, [fetch](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard-shared-with-dev.css) accepts additional callbacksfor advanced cases.
[*processEarlyHintsResponse*](https://resources.whatwg.org/standard.css "subtitle") is intended specifically for[responses](https://resources.whatwg.org/logo-fetch.svg "subtitle") whose [status](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is 103, and is currently handled only bynavigations.
[*processRequestBodyChunkLength*](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and[*processRequestEndOfBody*](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") notify the caller of request body uploadingprogress.
   
Note that the [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") operation starts in the same thread from which it was called,and then breaks off to run its internal operations [in parallel](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). The aforementioned callbacksare posted to a given [event loop](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") which is, by default, the[client](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")’s [global object](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). To processresponses [in parallel](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and handle interactions with the main thread by yourself,[fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with
[*useParallelQueue*](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") set to true.
   
### Manipulating an ongoing fetch
   
To manipulate a [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") operation that has already started, use the[fetch controller](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") returned by calling [fetch](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"). For example, you may[abort](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") the [fetch controller](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") due the user or page logic, or[terminate](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") it due to browser-internal circumstances.
   
In addition to terminating and aborting, callers may [report timing](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle")if this was not done automatically by passing the [initiator type](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle"), or[extract full timing info](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") and handle it on the caller side (this isdone only by navigations). The [fetch controller](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") is also used to[process the next manual redirect](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") for [requests](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") with[redirect mode](https://whatwg.org/ "subtitle") set to
"`manual`".

genLinkingSyntaxes(dfn) {    if(dfn.tagName != "DFN") return;    const type =
dfn.getAttribute('data-dfn-type');    if(!type) {        console.log(\`* doesn't 
have a data-dfn-type:\`, dfn);        return [];    }    // Return a function that 
wraps link text based on the type    const linkFormatter = linkFormatterFromType(type); 
   if(!linkFormatter) {        console.log(\`* has an unknown data-dfn-type:\`, dfn); 
       return [];    }    let ltAlts;    if(dfn.hasAttribute('data-lt')) {       
 ltAlts = dfn.getAttribute('data-lt')            .split("|")            .map(x=>x.trim()); 
   } else {        ltAlts = [dfn.textContent.trim()];    }    if(type == "type") 
{        // lt of "", but "foo" is the interior;        // > is how you write it 
with a for,        // not > or whatever.        for(var i = 0; i /.exec(lt);     
       if(match) { ltAlts[i] = match[1]; }        }    }    let forAlts;   
if(dfn.hasAttribute('data-dfn-for')) {        forAlts = dfn.getAttribute('data-dfn-for') 
           .split(",")            .map(x=>x.trim());    } else {        forAlts = 
[''];    }    let linkingSyntaxes = [];    if(!needsFor(type)) {        for(const 
lt of ltAlts) {            linkingSyntaxes.push(linkFormatter(lt));        }    } 
   if(!refusesFor(type)) {        for(const f of forAlts) {           
linkingSyntaxes.push(linkFormatter(\`${f}/${ltAlts[0]}\`))        }    }    return 
[        mk.b({}, 'Possible linking syntaxes:'),        mk.ul({},           
...linkingSyntaxes.map(link => {                const copyLink = async () =>    
                await navigator.clipboard.writeText(link);                return 
mk.li({},                    mk.div({ class: 'link-item' },                      
  mk.button({                            class: 'copy-icon', title: 'Copy',      
                      type: 'button',                            _onclick: copyLink, 
                           tabindex: 0,                        }, mk.span({ class: 
'icon' }) ),                        mk.span({}, link)                    )       
         );            })        )    ];}}
